The art school
Ground in masses: a short course by The ground student
What I teachHow I learnedNotebook

The working thread

Friday 25 September 2026, 23:58 (PDT), for 2 h 59 min. 280 steps, 121 images looked at.

This is the student's own thread: the brief it was given, what it wrote, each step it took, and every image it opened, shown as it saw it (possibly downscaled). Some of these images were later overwritten in the study folder, so this is the only place they survive. The raw record is the transcript.

The brief

You're a student learning to paint one thing beautifully, in the painterly pixel-art language of Mark Ferrari: the ground itself. That means earth, turf, gravel, forest floor and meadow floor as big, designed value masses, the stage everything else stands on.

Why this matters. A studio of students has been painting elements (cobbles, driftwood, willow, water, grass) in ~/work/pixelart-studies/. After 13 rounds of close comparison, their critic found one weakness common to all of them: the ground is texture everywhere at a single scale, with no big value masses. Uniform speckle under beautiful objects. Ferrari's ground is never like that. It's built from a few large shapes: shadowed banks, lit ledges, pools of light and dark, slopes turning. Texture lives inside those masses and follows them. The driftwood student proposed copying his near-ground masses (the ledges in Mountain Stream, V19; the banks in Mirror Pond, V16) as a starting point. The critic's full read is in ~/work/pixelart-studies/READY.md and elements/*/CRITIQUE.md.

The studio around you (read freely):

  • READY.md: techniques judged ready, and the recurring failures.
  • The pedagogy students' intermediate studies, their best work: pedagogy/master-copy/out/cmp_c2.png and cmp_c3.png (Ferrari's grass mound and bank: form classes across a mound), pedagogy/atelier/stage1–3 (value planning and form), and pedagogy/analysis/img (his value structure analysed: notans, value groups).
  • exemplars/: two student paintings with real magic.
  • Ferrari's indexed scenes: pedagogy/master-copy/ref/lw/ and aerial/refs/ferrari_lw/. The index maps are form maps, so you can read his ground's structure directly.
  • SYNTHESIS.md: notes on what earlier students found. Read it as inspiration, not a spec.

How the best students worked (take what helps; you own your approach):

  • Built up from the smallest form, one stage at a time, staying at each stage until it was truly convincing.
  • Started from a master copy of the closest Ferrari passage, reproduced by discovering the procedure and compared at 8×. Then moved toward their own subject a step at a time.
  • Close-read real photos: ground at 2 m, 10 m and 50 m, in sun and shade. Use python3 ~/work/pixelart-studies/tools/imgfetch.py "<query>" -n 12 -o <dir> --sheet.
  • Put work side by side at 8×: photo | theirs | Ferrari.

A useful outcome for the whole studio:

  • A way to lay down the big masses of any ground: value shapes driven by landform, light, shadow and moisture.
  • How each material's texture lives inside those masses: size, density and contrast following the mass.
  • How the result holds across distance.

The bar, stated plainly: ground that could sit beside Ferrari's at 8× and at a glance. "Improved" isn't done. Expect many iterations per stage, and honest "not there yet, here's why" notes are valued. Time and compute are generous.

Deliverables in ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/:

  • LESSONS.md with stage images and honest comparisons;
  • the technique as code with parameters (ground type, landform, light and hour, moisture, distance, seed);
  • labelled sheets;
  • a short "how other students can use this" note at the end. The cobbles, driftwood, willow and channel techniques are the obvious first users; if you can, show one of them sitting on your ground.

Read anywhere under ~/work/pixelart-studies/, write only in your folder. Resources: nice -n 10, at most 4 threads, OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 for numpy. If anything would help, say so in LESSONS.md.

The thread

# Ready to use: techniques from the studio (2026-09-25 night)

Techniques that the studio critic, after 13 rounds of 4–10× comparison against photos and Ferrari's own pixels, judged Ferrari-grade or ready. Each links to the student's work, which has the code and the lessons. Offered to the voxsim painting lanes as a resource, not a spec.

**Palette as light** (all students). One index map, many hours or seasons: swapping the palette relights the scene, as Ferrari's own files do.

- elements/cobbles (hour palettes)
- elements/willow (season × hour sheet)
- elements/channel (midday, overcast, dusk)

**Water** (elements/channel; LESSONS.md, src/technique.py; best image: out/s5_low_dusk_4x.png)

- Water is the sky's own ramp, indexed by reflected elevation, with checker as the water's material.
- Near edge light, far edge dark, with stepped bites.
- Reflections copy the painted pixels, then compress the values.
- A sheen band under the far bank.
- Far channels are one-row runs; detail comes from the row footprint.
- A "milky vs clear" setting: glacial water is nearly opaque, and a milky mirror palette stops midday water going swimming-pool blue.

**Stones and gravel** (elements/cobbles; cobbles_api.py; best: img/sheet_A_hour_x_distance.png)

- Darkness goes only in the pockets between stones, never as an outline.
- Stones are lopsided, faceted 3-D shapes, not ellipses.
- A stone → tick → grain distance ladder with no pop.
- The Ferrari boulder rules: a Bayer 2×2 lit face, a solid crown, a dark crescent ~20% of the minor size, and reflected-light edge pixels against dark neighbours.

**Cast shadows** (elements/willow, elements/driftwood). The ground itself, two steps darker, with its texture kept inside.

**Contact** (elements/willow). Grass overlapping the foot of a bush or stone.

**Willow leaves** (elements/willow). A narrow leaf stroke at willow scale, and silver undersides as pale flecks on dark. David thinks the willow may exceed the existing trees.

**Wood** (elements/driftwood; src/driftwood.py)

- A five-class cylinder shading order proven against Ferrari's own V09 trunk, with the lit band kept free of checker.
- Backlit rims.
- Grain, checks and knots drawn as clean runs.
- Piles separated by value.
- Wet wood.

**Grass zoom** (elements/grass-lod). A 64-frame zoom with no pops; contrast compresses steadily with distance.

**Far and iso** (aerial/; lib/tech_*.py; measure/). Measured canopy rules (crown spacing 2.2–2.9 lit-cap radii, lit share 0.35–0.56, conifers still read as upright spires), rivers from above as dark mirrors, and haze that lifts the black point and shrinks the value range.

## Not ready (from the critic)

- Cast shadows across stones.
- Riffles (a field of marks, not a few jets with plain mirror between).
- Braid bars from above (cut paper, not lobes).
- Evenly spaced grass f

# What earlier students discovered: notes on their craft

These are notes on the painstaking work of the students who came before you:

- four who each spent hours learning Ferrari's language on one small scene (`pedagogy/{atelier,master-copy,analysis,observation}`);
- seven who studied single topics.

They copied his pixels crop by crop, measured his grass against real grass, built a boulder up from a lit sphere, and diagrammed his picture before daring to paint it. What they found is here to inspire you and save you time. It isn't a specification. You'll see things they didn't, and some of what they wrote will turn out wrong or incomplete for your subject. Follow your own eyes. Their best work is in their intermediate studies (e.g. `master-copy/out/cmp_c*.png`, `atelier/stage2/`, `observation/studies/translate_*.png`, `analysis/img/`); those are worth looking at more than their final painters.

The tags below say how each finding came about:

- **[shown]**: a student built it and it worked.
- **[measured]**: a student measured it in Ferrari's pixels or in photos.
- **[mine]**: the integrator's reading, not yet tried by anyone.

Striking to us: the four students, working apart, found much the same underlying ideas.

## The convergence

Working alone, all four arms landed on the same architecture:

- **Paint indices, not colours.**
  - Master copy found that Ferrari's index map is literally a form map: indices are bins of surface direction, and "the palette is the light". The same index image serves morning and afternoon. [measured]
  - Atelier paints in "step space" along one material ramp. [shown]
  - Sky found that two of Ferrari's hour pairs are identical pixels. [measured]
  - Analysis found that value is the structure and colour is temperature and distance. [measured]
- **Two light families that never touch.** Atelier's terminator rule [shown], analysis's three-value picture plan with every mass kept inside its group [measured], and rock's lit and shadow groups [shown] are the same idea at two scales.
- **Dither mixes only adjacent steps of one ramp.** All four arms use it. Ordered 2×2 dither suits hard or precise things (rock, sky seams, water rows); random or diffusion dither suits organic texture (lawns, foam). Master copy measured this [measured]; analysis cites Ferrari's GDC talk for the same split.
- **The medium's own geometry.** Contours are clean runs (1:1, 2:1, 4-4-4 flats), measured on Ferrari's crests by analysis and atelier [measured]. Atelier redraws every major contour by snapping edges to clean ratios [shown]. Masses meet level ground or water in a dead-flat run with a 1-px darker contact line [measured: analysis, observation].
- **Clean runs are for narrow forms; mass boundaries are where texture ends.** David's correction (2026-09-25): the clean-run rule came from the *grass strokes*. It applies to anything n

Viewing tool: `_critic/zoom.py` (nearest-neighbour crops). Calibration crops of Ferrari natives: `cal_*.png`.

No stage images yet in any folder. cobbles/driftwood/willow have refs + scratch; channel has photos only. Calibrated on V19 rocks (checker midtones inside clean rock silhouettes, dark cast pockets between stones) and V09 bushes (2-4 px leaf clusters, dark gaps, 3-4 greens).

## Round 1 (2026-09-25 21:22)
Coordinator notes received: bar is Ferrari-indistinguishable at 8×; tone should be generous and specific, and the student owns the call. I kept a verdict line, framed as my read.

## Round 13 — 2026-09-25 23:50 — final sheets and "Where it stands" (finished)
**My closing read: your assessment is accurate, and your number 1 (the ground) is also my number 1.** The latest distance × seed sheet is your best work: the near piles read as silvered driftwood with pale root fans and end faces, the Morse check is gone, and at 16 and 6 px/m the pile holds as a readable cluster. (My read; your call.)

Ready to use:

- the cylinder class order (proven against his trunk), with the lit band kept clean of checker and the form turned by grain density
- contre-jour rims (stage 0B language)
- grain, checks and knots as projected clean-run curves on the 3-D surface
- value separation in piles: contact cracks, occlusion steps, and a mix of bleached and grey logs (`s5_grey_3x`, and distance × seed near)
- wet driftwood (dark brown with sharp glints)
- cast shadows as the ground two steps down

Not ready: the ground (texture, no masses: agreed, and it's the first thing anyone would catch); big logs at 8× (plateaus, and block-ended dashes); root fans too regular and too sparse on the root-wad sheet (two prongs read as a fork); pile intersections. Your proposed next study, a stage-0 copy of his near-ground masses (V19 ledges, V16 banks), is the right one. It would help every element student, not just you.
elements/grass-lod/CRITIQUE.md:23:1. **The stroke vocabulary is busier than his.** In `cmp_wip` his mound is a *flat* lit ochre ground with dark vertical strands (1 px wide, 2–6 tall) and a few mid-tone strands: two or three values, with most of the surface left flat, and the dark strands carrying all the texture. Yours mixes four or five greens plus a lemon yellow, with light dashes and dark dashes in equal measure, so it reads as camouflage. Try his economy: a flat lit base, dark strands as the grass, and light only as a paired highlight beside a dark gap (SYNTHESIS: the paired blade stroke).
elements/grass-lod/CRITIQUE.md-24-2. **Far contrast doesn't compress.** At 2 and 0.6 px/m the field is the same high-contrast yellow/olive stipple as at 8 px/m, just finer, so it reads as sponge or sand. Ferrari's far grass (`ref/v16_farbank_6x`, `ref/v04_farhill_5x`) drops most of its texture: near-flat bands of colour with sparse marks, and the shadow colour lifting toward the sky. With distance, the *share* of marks and their contrast should fall, not just their size.
elements/grass-lod/CRITIQUE.md-25-3. **The palette is acid.** The lit yellow is a lemon (almost 255,230,80) that no Ferrari grass uses. His lit grass is ochre or olive, and saturation drops with distance.
elements/grass-lod/CRITIQUE.md-26-4. **The zoom test needs smaller steps.** Your ladder jumps 4× at a time (30 → 8 → 2 → 0.6), and at that spacing any handoff will look like a pop, or hide one. For the handoff you're studying, render consecutive steps at about 1.25× apart across each mark-class boundary (e.g. 12, 10, 8, 6.5, 5 px/m) and put them side by side. I'll look at those for pops.
e

# output:
import re, json, numpy as np
def load(name):
    t=open(f'ref/lw/{name}.json').read()
    t=re.sub(r'([{,])\s*([A-Za-z_]+)\s*:', r'\1"\2":', t).replace("'",'"')
    d=json.loads(t)
    d['pal']=np.array(d['colors'],dtype=np.uint8)
    d['idx']=np.array(d['pixels'],dtype=np.int32).reshape(d['height'],d['width'])
    return d
"""Copy 3: the near, sun-lit grass bank (left foreground).  Ref crop native (0,250,150,219).

Discovered procedure (index map statistics, V16PM rows 254..473):

  * DEPTH PALETTES.  The bank is not one ramp; each depth band owns a tiny class set:
        far  (crest, y<48):   black-green, olive, halftone ochre, lit ochre   (4)
        mid  (48..140):       lit ochre + a *muted* olive (78) + a little mid (83)
        near (140..):         dark green (82), olive (83), lit ochre (84)
    The lit ochre is shared by all bands -- light is constant; what changes with distance
    is the shadow colour, which gets darker and greener toward the viewer.
  * GRAIN GROWS WITH NEARNESS.  Far: isotropic stipple (runs ~1.6 both ways).  Mid and
    near: 1px vertical strands, mean run 3.8 -> 5 px, horizontal runs stay ~1.5.
  * No lit-tip rule, no lean bias: transitions up/down are symmetric; blades are simply
    vertical 1px runs of colour whose class is drawn from the band's mixture.
  * Mixture drifts smoothly with depth (the value study): far crest has dark 'holes'
    (40% black-green), lit peaks just behind the crest, mid bank ~50% lit, near bank
    shifts to ~50% olive + ~30% dark green (self-shadowing in dense, close grass).
"""
import sys; sys.path.insert(0, 'src')
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import fl, compare

W, H = 150, 219
# classes (dusk colours)
C = {75: (28, 31, 20), 74: (60, 63, 45), 76: (106, 80, 40), 77: (196, 134, 76),
     78: (62, 59, 23), 82: (10, 34, 10), 83: (115, 92, 35), 84: (196, 131, 70)}
KEYS = list(C)
PAL = [C[k] for k in KEYS]
K = {k: i for i, k in enumerate(KEYS)}

# value study: class mixture keyed by depth row (copyist's measurement of masses)
STUDY = [  # y, {class: share}
    (0,   {75: .42, 77: .38, 74: .15, 76: .05}),
    (12,  {77: .65, 75: .22, 76: .10, 74: .03}),
    (24,  {77: .66, 76: .31, 75: .03}),
    (36,  {76: .53, 77: .40, 84: .05, 78: .02}),
    (48,  {76: .39, 78: .29, 84: .21, 77: .11}),
    (60,  {78: .45, 84: .35, 76: .12, 77: .08}),
    (72,  {84: .55, 78: .38, 83: .03, 82: .04}),
    (90,  {84: .44, 78: .26, 83: .21, 82: .09}),
    (120, {84: .52, 78: .23, 83: .22, 82: .03}),
    (144, {83: .47, 84: .45, 78: .07, 82: .01}),
    (170, {83: .50, 84: .34, 82: .14, 78: .02}),
    (195, {83: .48, 82: .24, 84: .28}),
    (219, {83: .46, 84: .32, 82: .22}),
]


def mixture_at(y, study=STUDY):
    ys = [s[0] for s in study]
    j = np.searchsorted(ys, y, side='right') - 1
    j = int(np.clip(j, 0, len(study) - 2))
    y0, a = study[j]; y1, b = study[j + 1]
    t = np.clip((y - y0) / (y1 - y0), 0, 1)
    keys = set(a) | set(b)
    return {k: (1 - t) * a.get(k, 0) + t * b.get(k, 0)
Bash: Create loader and render Ferrari scenes at 2x
mkdir -p ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/{src,ref,stages,sheets,photos} && cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground && cat > src/lw.py <<'EOF'
"""Load Ferrari LBM-json scenes (index map + palette)."""
import re, json, os, numpy as np
from PIL import Image
ROOTS = [os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/master-copy/ref/lw'),
         os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/aerial/refs/ferrari_lw')]
def load(name):
    for r in ROOTS:
        p = f'{r}/{name}.json'
        if os.path.exists(p):
            t = open(p).read(); break
    t = re.sub(r'([{,])\s*([A-Za-z_]+)\s*:', r'\1"\2":', t).replace("'", '"')
    d = json.loads(t)
    d['pal'] = np.array(d['colors'], dtype=np.uint8)
    d['idx'] = np.array(d['pixels'], dtype=np.int32).reshape(d['height'], d['width'])
    return d
def rgb(d, box=None):
    a = d['pal'][d['idx']]
    if box: x0, y0, x1, y1 = box; a = a[y0:y1, x0:x1]
    return a
def up(a, k):
    return np.repeat(np.repeat(a, k, 0), k, 1)
def save(a, path, k=1):
    Image.fromarray(up(np.asarray(a, np.uint8), k)).save(path)
EOF

# Learning to paint like Mark Ferrari: a study of *Alpine Tarn at Dusk*

Everything below comes from programs in `src/`, and every figure is in `img/`. I looked at each
figure myself before writing about it. The reference arrived as a 1268×938 PNG. It is an exact 2×
nearest-neighbour upscale (every 2×2 block is one colour, with no phase error), so the native
picture is **634×469 in 172 colours** (`img/00_native.png`). All measurements below are made on
the native pixels.

Contents

1. Analysis, drawn: planes, values, light, form, edges, clusters, palette, depth, pixel geometry
2. What it means: Ferrari's method, as a painter would teach it
3. The method: `src/paint/ferrari_method.py`, parameters and sheets
4. Checking my sheets with the same analyses, one by one
5. What I would do next

---

## 1. The analysis

### 1.1 Planes and big masses (`img/02_planes.png`)

I traced ten planes by hand, using polygons for the ground and colour tests to split sky from tree
and rock. Share of the picture:

| plane | area | | plane | area |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sky | 17.9% | | water | 16.5% |
| mountain | 4.0% | | left meadow (mid) | 4.6% |
| far forest | 9.0% | | left bank (fore) | 10.8% |
| framing trees | 15.7% | | right bank (fore) | 13.1% |
| far banks / hummocks | 6.6% | | island | 1.8% |

The composition is a **V of foreground banks opening onto water**. The lit left bank drops from the
middle-left down to the bottom centre, and the shadowed right bank rises diagonally from the bottom
centre to the right edge. The water sits in the V. Above it a band of low hummocks carries the far
shore, then a dark forest band, a small peak and a large sky. Tall framing conifers on both sides
close the rectangle. The peak, the only warm-and-cool jewel, sits almost dead centre, in a notch
the conifers leave open. Its reflection lies exactly under it, inside the V, so the V points the
eye at the mirrored peak.

### 1.2 Value structure (`img/03_grey.png`, `img/03_notan.png`, `img/03_valueplanes.png`, `img/03_valueplan.png`)

The grey version uses CIE L\*. Beside it is a squint version (Gaussian σ=2.5 px), because dithering
means individual pixels lie about the value a region reads as.

* **Two-value notan** (cut at L\*≈40): light = sky + snowfield + the reflected sky in the water +
  the lit left bank. Dark = every tree, the far banks and the whole right bank. It reads as an
  hourglass. A light field at the top pours through the notch around the peak into a light field
  at the bottom (water plus the left bank), pinched by two dark masses of trees and banks.
* **Three-value notan**, with k-means centres at L\* **16 / 42 / 70**. Every plane stays inside one
  or two of the three groups (squint median, then the 5–95% spread):

| plane | median | 5–95% | group |
|---|---|---|---|
| sky | 71 | 45–75 (the spread is only the tree edges) | light |
| mountain | 48 | 17–72 | all three: the one place that uses the full range |
| far forest | 20 | 9–44 | dark |
| framing trees |

# Lessons from the atelier: a boulder by a tarn at dusk, after Mark Ferrari

I worked value first and colour last. Each stage below says what I learned, points to the images, and says what went wrong. After the stages come the method (the program) and an honest comparison of my sheets with Ferrari's tarn.

Everything is in `~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/atelier/`. To regenerate every sheet, run `nice -n 10 python3 method.py sheets`: four single-thread worker processes, about 4 minutes.

**Research, and its limits.** I studied Ferrari's `ferrari_alpine_tarn_dusk.png` closely:

- palette statistics;
- crops at his native pixel scale (the file is his 634×469 frame doubled);
- measured run lengths of his contours.

I read his Q&A (EffectGames) on:

- palette economy;
- patterned dither for smooth ramps and diffusion dither for chaos;
- colour cycling.

On form and light I used Proko's value families (the terminator, core, reflected light that never reaches the halftone, cast shadow, occlusion). I also read Pedro Medeiros and Slynyrd on clustering and on rocks, and the notes on Kazuo Oga's poster-colour method (big soft masses first, detail last, about 21 colours).

I did **not** look closely at Ferrari's other colour-cycling scenes or at Ghibli plates. Saving images from the web counts as a download, which needs permissi
Bash: Make squint mass maps of Ferrari scenes
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && cat > analyze.py <<'EOF'
import lw, numpy as np, sys
from PIL import Image
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
def Lstar(rgb):
    c = rgb.astype(float)/255; c = np.where(c>0.04045, ((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4, c/12.92)
    Y = c@np.array([0.2126,0.7152,0.0722]); return np.where(Y>0.008856, 116*np.cbrt(Y)-16, 903.3*Y)
def squint(rgb, s=3): return ndi.gaussian_filter(Lstar(rgb), s)
if __name__=='__main__':
    for n in sys.argv[1:]:
        d=lw.load(n); a=lw.rgb(d); L=squint(a,3)
        q=np.digitize(L,np.percentile(L[240:],[25,50,75]))  # quartiles of the lower half
        g=(np.array([30,90,160,230])[q]).astype(np.uint8)
        out=np.concatenate([a,np.stack([g]*3,-1)],1)
        lw.save(out,f'../ref/mass_{n}.png',1)
EOF

"""crop.py NAME x0 y0 x1 y1 k [stretch] -> ../ref/crop_NAME_x0_y0_k.png"""
import lw, sys, numpy as np
n=sys.argv[1]; x0,y0,x1,y1,k=map(int,sys.argv[2:7]); st=len(sys.argv)>7
a=lw.rgb(lw.load(n))[y0:y1,x0:x1].astype(float)
if st:
    lo,hi=np.percentile(a,1),np.percentile(a,99.5); a=np.clip((a-lo)/(hi-lo),0,1)**0.8*255
out=f'../ref/crop_{n}_{x0}_{y0}_{k}{"s" if st else ""}.png'; lw.save(a,out,k); print(out)
EOF

# output:
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""imgfetch: fast, polite reference-image sourcing (Wikimedia Commons + Openverse).

  python3 imgfetch.py "cinder cone scoria slope" -n 12 -o refs/cinder
  python3 imgfetch.py -f queries.txt -n 8 -o refs            # one query per line; subdir per query
  options: --src commons,openverse   --width 1200   --sheet (writes <out>/_sheet.jpg)

Writes images plus <out>/sources.tsv (file, title, license, author, page url).
Cached: files already present are skipped. Handles 429/5xx with Retry-After backoff.
Parallel downloads (4 workers) with a shared per-host rate limiter (~3 req/s; Wikimedia's media
host is kept to 1 per 2 s and slows further on any 429, since it throttles hard per IP after bursts).
Openverse (mostly Flickr-hosted CC images) is the primary source because it stays fast.
"""
import argparse, concurrent.futures as cf, json, os, re, sys, threading, time, urllib.error, urllib.parse, urllib.request

UA = "pixelart-studies-imgfetch/1.0 (research; contact: local)"
_locks = {}; _last = {}; _glock = threading.Lock()

SLOW_HOSTS = {"upload.wikimedia.org": 2.0}   # Wikimedia media host: ~1 req/s or it 429s

def _throttle(host, min_gap=0.34):
    min_gap = SLOW_HOSTS.get(host, min_gap)
    with _glock:
        lk = _locks.setdefault(host, threading.Lock())
    with lk:
        dt = time.time() - _last.get(host, 0)
        if dt < min_gap:
            time.sleep(min_gap - dt)
        _last[host] = time.time()

def get(url, tries=4, timeout=25):
    host = urllib.parse.urlparse(url).netloc
    if host == "upload.wikimedia.org":
        tries = 2  # fail fast; other sources fill in
    for i in range(tries):
        _throttle(host)
        try:
            req = urllib.request.Request(url, headers={"User-Agent": UA})
            return urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout).read()
        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:

Bash: Write dither module and stage 0a islet copy
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && cat > dither.py <<'EOF'
"""Quantizers: continuous step coordinate v in [0, n-1] -> integer step, mixing only adjacent steps.

ordered(v, jitter): DPaint-style gradient-fill dither. 2x2 Bayer thresholds (so 50% = checker),
                    plus a random jitter on the threshold (DPaint's 'dither' slider).
"""
import numpy as np
B2 = np.array([[0.125, 0.625], [0.875, 0.375]])
B4 = (np.array([[0, 8, 2, 10], [12, 4, 14, 6], [3, 11, 1, 9], [15, 7, 13, 5]]) + 0.5) / 16

def tile(m, h, w, ox=0, oy=0):
    k = m.shape[0]
    return np.tile(m, (h // k + 2, w // k + 2))[oy % k: oy % k + h, ox % k: ox % k + w]

def ordered(v, jitter=0.25, rng=None, bayer=B2, n=None):
    h, w = v.shape
    rng = rng or np.random.default_rng(0)
    t = tile(bayer, h, w) + jitter * (rng.random((h, w)) - 0.5)
    base = np.floor(v)
    s = base + ((v - base) > t)
    if n is not None: s = np.clip(s, 0, n - 1)
    return s.astype(int)

def stochastic(v, rng=None, n=None, clump=None):
    """random threshold (optionally from a clumped noise field in [0,1])"""
    h, w = v.shape
    rng = rng or np.random.default_rng(0)
    t = rng.random((h, w)) if clump is None else clump
    base = np.floor(v)
    s = base + ((v - base) > t)
    if n is not None: s = np.clip(s, 0, n - 1)
    return s.astype(int)
EOF
cat > s0_islet.py <<'EOF'
"""Stage 0a: copy of V16's lit islet by procedure.
A: his value field (blurred step map) + my quantizer   -> isolates the dither procedure
B: my own field (DPaint contour-gradient with highlight point) + my quantizer -> full procedure"""
import lw, numpy as np, dither
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
d = lw.load('V16'); I = d['idx']; P = d['pal']
x0, y0, x1, y1 = 262, 262, 425, 305
S = I[y0:y1, x0:x1]
RAMP = [77, 76, 75, 74]            # step 0..3 (index order: 74 lit ... 77 darkest-cool)
rank = {k: i for i, k in enumerate(RAMP)}
mask = np.isin(S, RAMP)
st = np.vectorize(lambda k: rank.get(k, 0))(S).astype(float)
# his field: normalized blur of the step map inside the mask
sg = 2.5
num = ndi.gaussian_filter(st * mask, sg); den = ndi.gaussian_filter(mask.astype(float), sg) + 1e-6
field = num / den
rng = np.random.default_rng(1)
A = dither.ordered(field, jitter=0.35, rng=rng, n=4)

# B: my own field.  mask = his silhouette (we test fill, not drawing, here)
h, w = S.shape
yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w]
dist_in = ndi.distance_transform_edt(mask)
hx, hy = 8, 14                          # highlight point (upper-left, where the sun pool sits)
r = np.hypot((xx - hx) / 1.0, (yy - hy) * 2.2)   # oval falloff, squashed vertically (a flat-ish mound)
vB = 3.35 - r / 34.0                    # linear falloff from the highlight
vB = vB - 0.6 * np.exp(-dist_in / 2.0) * (yy > h * 0.55)   # darker foot rim at the waterline
vB = np.clip(vB, 0, 3)
B = dither.ordered(vB, jitter=0.35, rng=np.random.default_rng(2), n=4)

def paint(steps):
    out = P[S].copy()
    out[mask] = P[np.array(RAMP)[steps]][mask]
    return out
ref = P[S]
row = lambda *ims: np.concatenate([np.pad(i, ((0, 0), (0, 3), (0, 0))) for i in ims], 1)
lw.save(row(ref, paint(A), paint(B)), '../stages/s0a_islet_6x.png', 6)
# field diagnostic
g = lambda f: np.stack([(f / 3 * 255).astype(np.uint8)] * 3, -1)
lw.save(row(g(field), g(vB)), '../stages/s0a_islet_fields_4x.png', 4)
EOF
python3 s0_islet.py

# output:
elements/cobbles/cobbles_api.py
"""cobbles_api.py -- the technique as one call with parameters (stage 7).

paint_patch(W, H, rock, size_m, wet, density, sun, hour, px_per_stone, seed) -> RGB uint8

  rock          'granite' | 'greywacke' | 'basalt' | 'mixed' (a braided-river bar: mostly
                greywacke, some granite, some basalt)
  size_m        median clast size in metres (sets the lognormal spread's centre)
  px_per_stone  the median stone's size in PIXELS -- the distance knob.  >= 5: stones;
                2-5: ticks; < 2: grain
  wet           0 dry .. 1 all wet (stones on the wet ramp: darker, saturated, glints)
  density       packing: 1 = clast-supported (touching), < 1 opens matrix between
  sun           (azimuth, elevation) in degrees; hour picks the palette (the light's colour)
  seed          everything random
"""
import numpy as np
from bar import bar
from palette import water_ramp

MIXES = {'granite': [('granite', 1.0)], 'greywacke': [('greywacke', 1.0)], 'basalt': [('basalt', 1.0)],
         'mixed': [('greywacke', 0.55), ('granite', 0.25), ('basalt', 0.2)]}
HOUR_SUN = {'noon': (120, 62), 'morning': (30, 30), 'golden': (160, 16), 'dusk': (175, 7),
            'overcast': (100, 80)}


def paint_patch(W=96, H=64, rock='mixed', size_m=0.12, wet=0.0, density=1.0, sun=None, hour='noon',
                px_per_stone=16, seed=0, depth_ratio=1.0):
    sun = sun or HOUR_SUN[hour]
    ppm = px_per_stone / size_m
    cv, info = bar(W=W, H=H, sun=sun, seed=seed, head_m=size_m, tail_m=size_m,
                   px_per_m_far=ppm / depth_ratio, px_per_m_near=ppm, density=density, mix=MIXES[rock],
                   size_sd=0.4)
    if wet > 0:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 7)
        st = cv.mat >= 0
        cv.wet[st & (rng.random(cv.step.shape) < 1.0)] = 1 if wet >= 0.5 else 0
        if wet < 0.5 and info is not None:
            # damp: whole stones whose base lies in the lower part of the patch (per stone,
            # never a ruled line through a stone)
            cv.wet[:] = 0
            for i in info['order']:
                m = info['vis'][i]
                ys = np.nonzero(m)[0]
                if len(ys) and ys.max() > H * (1 - 2 * wet):
                    cv.wet[m] = 1
        g = cv.mat < 0
        cv.step[g] = np.maximum(cv.step[g] - int(round(wet * 2)), 0)
    return cv.render(hour)
elements/cobbles/cobbles_api.py:25:def paint_patch(W=96, H=64, rock='mixed', size_m=0.12, wet=0.0, density=1.0, sun=None, hour='noon',

Write: elements/ground/src/ground.py
"""ground.py -- the ground as big designed value masses, with texture living inside them.

    g = paint_ground(W, H, ground='turf', landform='rolling', hour='golden', sun=None,
                     moisture=0.2, ppm=24, pitch=14, cam_h=1.7, canopy=0.0, seed=0)
    g.rgb      (H, W, 3) uint8
    g.mat, g.band, g.step   the index canvas (material, depth band, ramp step) -- palette-swappable
    g.depth, g.wx, g.wz, g.h   per-pixel world buffers, so other students can stand objects on it
    g.relight(hour)         new rgb from the same index canvas

Layers (each a separate function so it can be tuned against a reference):
  1 LANDFORM   analytic height h(x,z) in metres built from a few big features
               (rolling swells, hummocks, bank, ledges, path) -- the masses come from here
  2 RENDER     voxel-space column march: per pixel world x,z,h,normal,depth,px-per-metre
  3 LIGHT      sun visibility (terrain horizon shadow x off-frame canopy shadow) and N.L,
               sky ambient, hollow occlusion; *smoothed at mass scale* so light makes shapes
  4 VALUE PLAN two families that never touch: shadow steps 0..2, light steps 3..5; the
               terminator is a jump. crest separation: land just behind a crest drops a step.
  5 MATERIAL   turf / meadow / earth / gravel / forest floor; path and bare risers; moisture
  6 MARKS      offsets in step space whose size follows px-per-metre and whose contrast follows
               the family and distance: strands -> ticks -> DPaint dither -> flat
  7 QUANTIZE   ordered 2x2 + jitter (the V16 islet recipe), adjacent steps only
  8 PALETTE    per material x depth band x hour ramp, generated from albedo x light colour
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
import dither

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 1 LANDFORM
class Landform:
    """h(x, z) as a sum of big features. Everything analytic, so it can be sampled anywhere."""
    def __init__(self, kind='rolling', seed=0, relief=1.0, extent=(-200, 200, 0, 400), path=False,
                 water=None):
        self.kind, self.seed, self.relief = kind, seed, relief
        r = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        self.r = r
        # low-frequency swells: random-direction sinusoids (smooth, big)
        n = 7
        ang = r.uniform(0, np.pi, n)
        wl = r.uniform(18, 60, n)
        self.sw = [(np.cos(a) * 2 * np.pi / l, np.sin(a) * 2 * np.pi / l, r.uniform(0, 6.3), l / 60.0)
                   for a, l in zip(ang, wl)]
        # micro relief (only drives marks/light breakup, small amplitude)
        m = 9
        ang = r.uniform(0, np.pi, m); wl = r.uniform(1.5, 6, m)
        self.micro = [(np.cos(a) * 2 * np.pi / l, np.sin(a) * 2 * np.pi / l, r.uniform(0, 6.3), l / 6.0)
                      for a, l in zip(ang, wl)]
        # hummocks: lozenges
        self.hum = []
        if kind in ('hummocks', 'bank'):
            for _ in range(60):
                self.hum.append((r.uniform(-60, 60), r.uniform(3, 140), r.uniform(3, 9), r.uniform(1.5, 3.5),
                                 r.uniform(0.4, 1.3), r.uniform(-0.5, 0.5)))
        self.path = path
        self.path_a, self.path_b, self.path_c = r.uniform(2, 5), r.uniform(12, 25), r.uniform(-1.5, 1.5)
        self.water = water

    def path_center(self, z):
        return self.path_c + self.path_a * np.sin(z / self.path_b + 1.3) + 0.02 * z

    def path_dist(self, x, z):
        return np.abs(x - self.path_center(z))

    def h(self, x, z, micro=True):
        k = self.kind
        H = np.zeros(np.broadcast(x, z).shape)
        amp = {'flat': 0.25, 'rolling': 1.6, 'hummocks': 0.5, 'bank': 0.6, 'ledges': 0.4}[k] * self.relief
        for kx, kz, ph, a in self.sw:
            H = H + amp * a * np.sin(kx * x + kz * z + ph)
        if k == 'rolling':
            # rolling land swells more with distance (terraces of V13)
            pass
        if k in ('hummocks', 'bank'):
            for cx, cz, ax, az, ht, rot in self.hum:
                c, s = np.cos(rot), np.sin(rot)
                u = ((x - cx) * c + (z - cz) * s) / ax
                v = (-(x - cx) * s + (z - cz) * c) / az
                q = u * u + v * v
                H = H + ht * self.relief * np.where(q < 1, (1 - q) ** 1.5, 0)
        if k == 'bank':
            # the land falls toward a shoreline at z = zw (the viewer stands on a bank)
            zw = self.water if self.water is not None else 14.0
            H = H - 1.8 * self.relief * (1 / (1 + np.exp(-(z - zw) / 2.5)))
        if k == 'ledges':
            # a slope rising away from the viewer, cut into terraces with risers facing us
            base = 0.18 * z
            step = 2.4 * self.relief
            q = base / step
            fr = q - np.floor(q)
            riser = 1 / (1 + np.exp(-(fr - 0.82) * 22))
            H = H + step * (np.floor(q) + riser) + 0.4 * np.sin(x / 9 + np.floor(q) * 2.1)
        if micro:
            for kx, kz, ph, a in self.micro:
                H = H + 0.05 * a * np.sin(kx * x + kz * z + ph)
        if self.path:
            d = self.path_dist(x, z)
            H = H - 0.12 * np.exp(-(d / 1.0) ** 2)
        return H

    def normal(self, x, z, e=0.15, micro=True):
        hx = (self.h(x + e, z, micro) - self.h(x - e, z, micro)) / (2 * e)
        hz = (self.h(x, z + e, micro) - self.h(x, z - e, micro)) / (2 * e)
        n = np.stack([-hx, np.ones_like(hx), -hz], -1)
        return n / np.linalg.norm(n, axis=-1, keepdims=True)


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 2 RENDER
def render(land, W, H, ppm=24, pitch=14, cam_h=1.7, steps=900):
    """Column march. ppm = pixels per metre at the ground point under the screen centre."""
    p = np.radians(pitch)
    zc0 = cam_h / np.sin(p)                    # camera-space depth of the centre ground point
    F = ppm * zc0
    cx, cy = W / 2, H / 2
    cols = np.arange(W) + 0.5
    rows = np.arange(H)[:, None] + 0.5
    # nearest ground: ray through the bottom row
    ang_bot = p + np.arctan((H - cy) / F)
    z_near = max(0.05, cam_h / np.tan(min(ang_bot, 1.5)) * 0.7)
    ang_top = p - np.arctan(cy / F)
    z_far = 2000.0 if ang_top <= 0.002 else min(2000.0, cam_h / np.tan(ang_top) * 3 + 50)
    zs = z_near * (z_far / z_near) ** (np.arange(steps) / (steps - 1))
    Z = np.full((H, W), np.inf); X = np.zeros((H, W))
    ymin = np.full(W, H + 0.0)
    for z in zs:
        zc_est = z * np.cos(p) + cam_h * np.sin(p)
        x = (cols - cx) * zc_est / F
        h = land.h(x, np.full_like(x, z))
        y = h - cam_h
        zc = -y * np.sin(p) + z * np.cos(p)
        yc = y * np.cos(p) + z * np.sin(p)
        row = cy - F * yc / np.maximum(zc, 1e-3)
        m = (rows >= row[None, :]) & (rows < ymin[None, :])
        if m.any():
            Z = np.where(m, z, Z)
            X = np.where(m, x[None, :], X)
            ymin = np.minimum(ymin, row)
    sky = ~np.isfinite(Z)
    Zs = np.where(sky, z_far, Z)
    buf = dict(z=Zs, x=X, sky=sky, F=F, pitch=p, cam_h=cam_h)
    zc = Zs * np.cos(p) + cam_h * np.sin(p)
    buf['ppm'] = F / zc                         # local pixels per metre
    buf['h'] = land.h(X, Zs)
    buf['n'] = land.normal(X, Zs)
    buf['n_macro'] = land.normal(X, Zs, e=0.8, micro=False)
    return buf


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 3 LIGHT
HOURS = {
    #          sun az, el   sun colour         sky/shade colour      haze colour
    'noon':    (35, 58, (1.00, 0.97, 0.88), (0.42, 0.55, 0.80), (0.70, 0.80, 0.92)),
    'morning': (-30, 24, (1.00, 0.90, 0.72), (0.40, 0.50, 0.75), (0.78, 0.80, 0.88)),
    'golden':  (20, 14, (1.00, 0.72, 0.42), (0.36, 0.40, 0.66), (0.90, 0.72, 0.58)),
    'dusk':    (70, 5, (0.95, 0.52, 0.40), (0.34, 0.30, 0.52), (0.70, 0.52, 0.62)),
    'overcast': (40, 70, (0.62, 0.64, 0.66), (0.55, 0.58, 0.63), (0.74, 0.76, 0.78)),
}

def sun_vec(az, el):
    """az: 0 = sun on the left of the frame, 90 = behind the scene (contre-jour), -90 = behind camera."""
    a, e = np.radians(az), np.radians(el)
    return np.array([-np.cos(a) * np.cos(e), np.sin(e), np.sin(a) * np.cos(e)])

def canopy_field(x, z, cover, scale, sunv, seed):
    """Shadows of off-frame trees: soft union of crown shadows, stretched along the sun's azimuth
    (longer when the sun is low). Returns sun visibility 0..1 (continuous; the dither makes edges)."""
    if cover <= 0:
        return np.ones_like(x)
    r = np.random.default_rng(seed + 991)
    n = 900
    cxs = r.uniform(-120, 120, n); czs = r.uniform(-10, 400, n)
    rad = scale * r.uniform(0.5, 1.4, n)
    sx, sz = sunv[0], sunv[2]
    hl = np.hypot(sx, sz) + 1e-6
    ux, uz = sx / hl, sz / hl                        # horizontal sun direction
    stretch = np.clip(1 / np.tan(np.arcsin(np.clip(sunv[1], 0.05, 1))), 1, 4)
    acc = np.zeros_like(x)
    for cx, cz, rr in zip(cxs, czs, rad):
        dx, dz = x - cx, z - cz
        if np.abs(dz).min() > rr * 5 and np.abs(dx).min() > rr * 5:
            continue
        a = (dx * ux + dz * uz) / (rr * stretch)
        b = (-dx * uz + dz * ux) / rr
        acc = acc + np.exp(-(a * a + b * b) * 1.5)
    # threshold so that 'cover' of the ground is in shade; soft band gives dither in the edge
    thr = np.quantile(acc, 1 - cover) if cover < 1 else acc.min() - 1
    return 1 - 1 / (1 + np.exp(-(acc - thr) / 0.08))

def light(land, buf, az, el, canopy=0.0, canopy_scale=3.0, seed=0, mass_blur=1.2):
    s = sun_vec(az, el)
    n = buf['n']; nm = buf['n_macro']
    X, Z, Hh = buf['x'], buf['z'], buf['h']
    ndl_macro = np.clip((nm * s).sum(-1), 0, 1)
    ndl_micro = np.clip((n * s).sum(-1), 0, 1)
    # terrain horizon shadow: march toward the sun
    vis = np.ones_like(X)
    hor = np.hypot(s[0], s[2]) + 1e-6
    tan_el = s[1] / hor
    for t in [0.5, 1, 2, 3.5, 5.5, 8, 12, 18, 26, 38, 55, 80]:
        xs = X + s[0] / hor * t; zs = Z + s[2] / hor * t
        hs = land.h(xs, zs, micro=False)
        occl = hs - (Hh + tan_el * t)
        vis = np.minimum(vis, np.clip(0.5 - occl / (0.08 + 0.02 * t), 0, 1))
    can = canopy_field(X, Z, canopy, canopy_scale, s, seed)
    sunvis = vis * can
    # hollow occlusion: height below the local mean (large-scale) -> less sky
    hl = land.h(X, Z, micro=False)
    ring = np.mean([land.h(X + dx, Z + dz, micro=False) for dx, dz in
                    [(3, 0), (-3, 0), (0, 3), (0, -3), (2, 2), (-2, 2), (2, -2), (-2, -2)]], 0)
    hollow = np.clip((ring - hl) / 0.5, -1, 1)
    sky = np.clip(0.55 + 0.45 * n[..., 1] - 0.25 * hollow, 0, 1)
    return dict(ndl=ndl_macro, ndl_micro=ndl_micro, sunvis=sunvis, terrain_vis=vis, canopy=can,
                sky=sky, hollow=hollow, s=s)


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 4 VALUE PLAN
def value_plan(buf, L, key='golden', sep=0.9, sheen=0.5, lit_floor=0.10):
    """Continuous step coordinate v in [0, 5.99]: shadow family 0..2.99, light family 3..5.99."""
    direct = L['sunvis'] * L['ndl']
    # a flat plane at this sun takes N.L = s_y; normalize so flat ground sits mid-light family
    sy = max(L['s'][1], 0.06)
    rel = np.clip(direct / max(sy * 1.35, 0.12), 0, 1.25)
    lit = (L['sunvis'] > 0.5) & (L['ndl'] > lit_floor * sy)
    # light family: 3.0 .. 5.99 ; strongly turned toward the sun -> top step
    v_lit = 3.0 + 2.99 * np.clip(rel ** 0.8, 0, 1)
    # shadow family: 0 .. 2.99 from sky ambient; faces turned to the sun but shadowed (cast)
    # keep a little reflected light (they are the 'lit' plane in shadow) -> v up to 2.7
    v_sh = 0.25 + 2.3 * L['sky'] + 0.4 * np.clip(L['ndl'] / sy, 0, 1) * (1 - L['terrain_vis'] * L['canopy'])
    v_sh = np.clip(v_sh - 0.5, 0, 2.99)
    v = np.where(lit, v_lit, v_sh)
    # the soft canopy edge: blend across the family boundary only inside the penumbra
    pen = (L['sunvis'] > 0.15) & (L['sunvis'] < 0.85) & (L['ndl'] > lit_floor * sy)
    t = np.clip((L['sunvis'] - 0.15) / 0.7, 0, 1)
    v = np.where(pen, (1 - t) * v_sh + t * v_lit, v)
    # grazing sheen: ground brightens as it recedes (Ferrari's receding planes, V16 banks)
    d = buf['z']
    v = v + sheen * np.clip(np.log(d / 3) / 4, 0, 1) * (v >= 3)
    # crest separation: land just behind (above on screen) a crest silhouette drops a step
    Hh, W = v.shape
    z = buf['z']
    jump = np.zeros_like(v, bool)
    jump[:-1] = (z[:-1] > z[1:] * 1.25) & ~buf['sky'][:-1]
    k = 0
    sepm = np.zeros_like(v)
    cur = jump.copy()
    for k in range(1, 7):
        sepm = np.maximum(sepm, cur * (1 - k / 7))
        cur = np.roll(cur, -1, 0); cur[-1] = False
    v = v - sep * sepm
    return np.clip(v, 0, 5.99), lit, sepm


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 5 MATERIAL
MATS = ['turf', 'meadow', 'earth', 'gravel', 'forest', 'moss', 'water']
MI = {m: i for i, m in enumerate(MATS)}

def materials(land, buf, ground, moisture, L, seed=0):
    X, Z = buf['x'], buf['z']
    mat = np.full(X.shape, MI[ground], int)
    slope = 1 - buf['n_macro'][..., 1]
    wet = np.clip(moisture + 0.6 * np.clip(L['hollow'], 0, 1) - 0.15, 0, 1)
    if ground in ('turf', 'meadow'):
        mat[slope > 0.22] = MI['earth']          # bare risers and cut banks
    if land.path:
        pd = land.path_dist(X, Z)
        # ragged edge: the path's width breathes and its border is broken by the grass
        r = np.random.default_rng(seed + 5)
        edge = 0.75 + 0.15 * np.sin(Z * 1.7 + 1) + 0.1 * np.sin(Z * 4.3 + X)
        mat[pd < edge] = MI['earth']
    if ground == 'forest':
        mat[(wet > 0.45) & (L['canopy'] < 0.7)] = MI['moss']
        mat[(wet > 0.6)] = MI['moss']
    # puddles: earth in hollows when wet
    puddle = (mat == MI['earth']) & (wet > 0.72) & (L['hollow'] > 0.25)
    mat[puddle] = MI['water']
    if land.water is not None:
        mat[buf['h'] < -1.2] = MI['water']
    return mat, wet


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 6 MARKS
def runs_field(Hh, W, runlen, rng, vertical=True, amp=None):
    """Random offsets constant along runs (strands if vertical, streaks if horizontal).
    runlen: per-pixel mean run length (px). Returns offsets ~ N(0,1) per run."""
    out = np.zeros((Hh, W))
    if not vertical:
        return runs_field(W, Hh, runlen.T, rng, True).T
    for x in range(W):
        y = Hh - 1
        col = runlen[:, x]
        while y >= 0:
            L = max(1, int(round(col[y] * rng.uniform(0.45, 1.4))))
            o = rng.normal()
            out[max(0, y - L + 1): y + 1, x] = o
            y -= L
    return out

def stones_field(buf, size_m, rng, density=0.7):
    """Gravel / litter: small blobs with a light top row and a dark lower rim (offsets)."""
    Hh, W = buf['z'].shape
    out = np.zeros((Hh, W))
    ppm = buf['ppm']
    n = int(Hh * W * density / 6)
    ys = rng.integers(0, Hh, n); xs = rng.integers(0, W, n)
    order = np.argsort(ys)
    for i in order:
        y, x = ys[i], xs[i]
        s = size_m * ppm[y, x] * rng.uniform(0.6, 1.5)
        if s < 1.6:
            out[y, x] += rng.choice([-1, 1]) * 0.8
            continue
        a = max(1, int(round(s / 2))); b = max(1, int(round(s / 3.2)))
        y0, y1 = max(0, y - b), min(Hh, y + b + 1)
        x0, x1 = max(0, x - a), min(W, x + a + 1)
        yy, xx = np.mgrid[y0:y1, x0:x1]
        q = ((xx - x) / (a + 0.5)) ** 2 + ((yy - y) / (b + 0.5)) ** 2
        m = q < 1
        tone = rng.normal(0.3, 0.5)
        top = (yy - y) < -b * 0.2
        bot = (yy - y) > b * 0.4
        blk = np.where(top, tone + 0.8, np.where(bot, tone - 0.5, tone))
        sub = out[y0:y1, x0:x1]
        sub[m] = blk[m]
        # pocket under the stone
        yb = min(Hh - 1, y + b + 1)
        out[yb, x0:x1][np.abs(np.arange(x0, x1) - x) < a * 0.8] = -1.3
    return out

def marks(buf, mat, v, lit, rng, contrast=1.0):
    """Offsets in step space. Size follows px-per-metre; contrast follows family & distance."""
    Hh, W = v.shape
    ppm = buf['ppm']
    off = np.zeros((Hh, W))
    # grass blades: vertical strands, height ~0.22 m turf / 0.5 m meadow
    for m, bh_m in (('turf', 0.2), ('meadow', 0.45), ('moss', 0.05)):
        sel = mat == MI[m]
        if not sel.any():
            continue
        bh = bh_m * ppm
        rl = np.clip(bh * 0.9, 1, 14)
        o = runs_field(Hh, W, rl, rng, True)
        # contrast grows with nearness (blade px), strong in light, weaker in shadow
        a = np.clip((bh - 0.6) / 4.0, 0.12, 1.0) * np.where(lit, 0.95, 0.6)
        # in light: marks are mostly *dark* (strands and gaps on a lit base) -- Ferrari's economy
        o = np.where(lit, np.where(o < 0.35, o * 1.25, o * 0.45), o)
        off[sel] = (a * o)[sel]
    # earth: horizontal streaks (ruts, the grain of the ground plane) + pebbles
    sel = mat == MI['earth']
    if sel.any():
        rl = np.clip(0.5 * ppm, 1, 18)
        o = runs_field(Hh, W, rl, rng, False)
        a = np.clip((0.3 * ppm - 0.5) / 4, 0.1, 0.7)
        st = stones_field(buf, 0.035, rng, density=0.18)
        off[sel] = (a * o * 0.8 + 0.8 * np.clip(0.03 * ppm, 0.15, 1) * st)[sel]
    sel = mat == MI['gravel']
    if sel.any():
        st = stones_field(buf, 0.06, rng, density=0.9)
        off[sel] = (np.clip(0.05 * ppm / 2, 0.2, 1) * st)[sel]
    sel = mat == MI['forest']
    if sel.any():
        st = stones_field(buf, 0.05, rng, density=0.8)       # leaf litter clumps
        rl = np.clip(0.08 * ppm, 1, 6)
        o = runs_field(Hh, W, rl, rng, True) * 0.4
        off[sel] = (np.clip(0.05 * ppm / 1.5, 0.2, 1) * st * np.where(lit, 1.0, 0.55) + o)[sel]
    return off * contrast


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 8 PALETTE
ALBEDO = {  # linear-ish albedo, hand-picked; hue drift across the ramp added below
    'turf': (0.20, 0.30, 0.07), 'meadow': (0.36, 0.33, 0.10), 'earth': (0.34, 0.22, 0.13),
    'gravel': (0.34, 0.32, 0.29), 'forest': (0.26, 0.17, 0.09), 'moss': (0.18, 0.30, 0.05),
    'water': (0.5, 0.5, 0.5)}

def srgb(c):
    c = np.clip(c, 0, 1)
    return np.where(c <= 0.0031308, 12.92 * c, 1.055 * c ** (1 / 2.4) - 0.055)

def ramp(mat, hour, band=0, wet=0.0, nb=3):
    """6 steps: 0 core/deep shadow, 1 shadow, 2 reflected/halftone-in-shadow | 3 halftone, 4 light, 5 top light."""
    az, el, sunc, skyc, haze = HOURS[hour]
    alb = np.array(ALBEDO[mat])
    if wet > 0:
        alb = alb * (1 - 0.45 * wet) ** 1.0
        alb = alb ** (1 + 0.25 * wet) * (1 + 0.4 * wet)   # saturate a little
    sunc, skyc, haze = map(np.array, (sunc, skyc, haze))
    if mat == 'water':
        c = [haze * k for k in (0.35, 0.5, 0.62, 0.75, 0.88, 1.0)]
    else:
        shade = [alb * skyc * k for k in (0.05, 0.13, 0.24)]
        litv = [alb * (sunc * k + skyc * 0.18) for k in (0.55, 0.85, 1.15)]
        # the top light shifts warm/yellow (the lit ochre of V16PM, the yellow islet of V16)
        litv[2] = litv[2] * np.array([1.08, 1.05, 0.8])
        # the core shifts cool-dark (the teal darks of V16)
        shade[0] = shade[0] * np.array([0.7, 0.95, 1.4])
        c = shade + litv
    c = np.array(c)
    out = srgb(c * 2.2)
    # depth band: far bands lift the black point toward the haze and compress
    f = [0.0, 0.22, 0.45][band]
    out = out * (1 - f) + srgb(haze * 0.8)[None] * f * np.linspace(1.0, 0.5, 6)[:, None]
    return (np.clip(out, 0, 1) * 255).astype(np.uint8)


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ assemble
class Ground:
    pass

def paint_ground(W=320, H=200, ground='turf', landform='rolling', hour='golden', sun=None,
                 moisture=0.2, ppm=24, pitch=14, cam_h=1.7, canopy=0.0, canopy_scale=3.0,
                 path=False, relief=1.0, sep=0.9, sheen=0.5, contrast=1.0, jitter=0.5,
                 band_z=(12, 45), seed=0, water=None):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    land = Landform(landform, seed, relief, path=path, water=water)
    buf = render(land, W, H, ppm, pitch, cam_h)
    az, el = (sun if sun else HOURS[hour][:2])
    L = light(land, buf, az, el, canopy, canopy_scale, seed)
    v, lit, sepm = value_plan(buf, L, hour, sep=sep, sheen=sheen)
    mat, wet = materials(land, buf, ground, moisture, L, seed)
    # moisture darkens the mass (a step at full wet) -- a mass-level change, not a texture
    v = v - 0.9 * wet * (v > 0.5)
    off = marks(buf, mat, v, lit, rng, contrast)
    # distance: mark contrast compresses with depth beyond the near band
    fade = np.clip(1.2 - np.log(buf['z'] / band_z[0] + 1e-6) / 2.2, 0.15, 1)
    vv = v + off * fade
    # keep the families apart: marks may not cross the terminator (only a thin fringe may)
    vv = np.where(lit, np.clip(vv, 2.6, 5.99), np.clip(vv, 0, 3.1))
    step = dither.ordered(vv, jitter=jitter, rng=rng, n=6)
    band = np.digitize(buf['z'], band_z)
    mat[buf['sky']] = -1
    g = Ground()
    g.__dict__.update(mat=mat, band=band, step=step, v=v, vv=vv, lit=lit, wet=wet, buf=buf, L=L,
                      land=land, depth=buf['z'], wx=buf['x'], wz=buf['z'], h=buf['h'], sepm=sepm,
                      hour=hour, moisture=moisture)
    g.relight = lambda hr, g=g: colour(g, hr)
    g.rgb = colour(g, hour)
    return g

def colour(g, hour, sky_rgb=None):
    Hh, W = g.step.shape
    out = np.zeros((Hh, W, 3), np.uint8)
    az, el, sunc, skyc, haze = HOURS[hour]
    for mi, m in enumerate(MATS):
        for b in range(3):
            for wetk, wsel in ((0, g.wet < 0.5), (1, g.wet >= 0.5)):
                sel = (g.mat == mi) & (g.band == b) & wsel
                if sel.any():
                    R = ramp(m, hour, b, wet=0.8 * wetk if g.moisture > 0 else 0)
                    out[sel] = R[g.step[sel]]
    sky = g.mat < 0
    if sky.any():
        out[sky] = (srgb(np.array(haze) * 0.9) * 255).astype(np.uint8)
    return out

import re
s=open('ground.py').read()
s=s.replace("H = H + ht * self.relief * np.where(q < 1, (1 - q) ** 1.5, 0)","H = H + ht * self.relief * np.clip(1 - q, 0, None) ** 1.5")

    v_lit = 3.0 + 3.0 * np.clip(0.42 + 0.85 * (r - 1), 0, 0.999)""")
# palette by L* targets
old=s[s.index("def ramp(mat"):s.index("# ------------------------------------------------------------------ assemble")]
new='''LSTEP = {  # target CIE L* per step: few steps, far apart; darks near black (V16 / V16PM ramps)
    'turf':   (4, 11, 21, 34, 49, 66),
    'meadow': (6, 14, 25, 40, 56, 72),
    'earth':  (7, 15, 25, 37, 50, 63),
    'gravel': (8, 17, 28, 42, 57, 72),
    'forest': (4, 10, 19, 31, 45, 60),
    'moss':   (5, 12, 22, 36, 52, 70),
    'water':  (30, 40, 50, 60, 70, 80)}

def _lin(c):
    c = np.clip(c, 0, 1)
    return np.where(c <= 0.04045, c / 12.92, ((c + 0.055) / 1.055) ** 2.4)

def _Y2L(Y):
    return np.where(Y > 0.008856, 116 * np.cbrt(Y) - 16, 903.3 * Y)

def _L2Y(L):
    return np.where(L > 8, ((L + 16) / 116) ** 3, L / 903.3)

def _set_L(rgb_lin, L):
    """scale a linear colour to hit luminance L* (clipping pushes toward white gracefully)"""
    Y = (rgb_lin * np.array([0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722])).sum()
    c = rgb_lin * (_L2Y(L) / max(Y, 1e-6))
    for _ in range(6):                                   # redistribute clipped energy
        over = np.clip(c - 1, 0, None).sum()
        c = np.clip(c, 0, 1)
        if over < 1e-4: break
        c = c + (1 - c) * over / 3
    return c

def ramp(mat, hour, band=0, wet=0.0):
    """6 steps: 0 core, 1 shadow, 2 reflected light | 3 halftone, 4 light, 5 top light."""
    az, el, sunc, skyc, haze = HOURS[hour]
    alb = np.array(ALBEDO[mat]); sunc, skyc, haze = map(np.array, (sunc, skyc, haze))
    Ls = np.array(LSTEP[mat], float)
    # the light family's value follows the light's strength (dusk and overcast are low-key)
    key = {'noon': 1.0, 'morning': 0.95, 'golden': 0.92, 'dusk': 0.72, 'overcast': 0.82}[hour]
    Ls[3:] = Ls[3:] * key
    if hour == 'overcast':
        Ls[:3] = Ls[:3] + np.array([6, 6, 4])            # soft light: shadows open up, no terminator
    if wet > 0:
        Ls = Ls * (1 - 0.3 * wet)
        alb = alb ** (1 + 0.6 * wet)                     # darker and more saturated
    out = []
    for i in range(6):
        if mat == 'water':
            c = haze * (0.8 + 0.05 * i)
        elif i < 3:
            c = alb * skyc * (1.0 if i else 0.9)
            if i == 0: c = c * np.array([0.75, 0.95, 1.35])   # cool core (V16's teal darks)
        else:
            c = alb * (sunc + skyc * 0.15)
            if i == 5: c = c * np.array([1.12, 1.06, 0.62])    # top light goes yellow/ochre
            if i == 3: c = c * 0.7 + alb * skyc * 0.6          # halftone keeps some sky
        out.append(_set_L(c, Ls[i]))
    out = np.array(out)
    f = [0.0, 0.18, 0.4][band]
    hz = _lin(np.array([0, 0, 0]) + 0) + haze * 0.55
    w = f * np.linspace(1.0, 0.35, 6)[:, None]              # far: black point lifts toward haze
    out = out * (1 - w) + hz[None] * w
    return (np.clip(srgb(out), 0, 1) * 255).astype(np.uint8)


def palette_sheet(k=12):
    rows = []
    for hr in HOURS:
        row = []
        for m in MATS:
            for b in range(3):
                row.append(np.repeat(ramp(m, hr, b)[None], 1, 0))
            row.append(np.zeros((1, 1, 3), np.uint8))
        rows.append(np.concatenate(row, 1))
    return np.concatenate(rows, 0)

s=s.replace(old,new)
# pass big shadow ("shade") as a second canopy field in light()
s=s.replace("def light(land, buf, az, el, canopy=0.0, canopy_scale=3.0, seed=0, mass_blur=1.2):",
 "def light(land, buf, az, el, canopy=0.0, canopy_scale=3.0, seed=0, shade=0.0, shade_scale=12.0):")

new='''def crest_fringe(step, mat, band, buf, lit, rng, tip=0.2, holes=0.3):
    """Blade tips break the top of every crest: the near mass's grass pokes up over the land
    behind it (lit peaks), and dark holes open along the crest line (V16PM C2/C3 mound)."""
    z = buf['z']; Hh, W = z.shape
    ppm = buf['ppm']
    crest = np.zeros_like(z, bool)
    crest[1:] = (z[:-1] > z[1:] * 1.25) | buf['sky'][:-1]
    crest &= ~buf['sky']
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(crest)
    for y, x in zip(ys, xs):
        m = mat[y, x]
        if m not in (MI['turf'], MI['meadow'], MI['moss'], MI['forest']):
            continue
        bh = tip * (2.2 if m == MI['meadow'] else 1) * ppm[y, x]
        if bh < 0.8:
            continue
        u = rng.random()
        if u < holes:
            step[y, x] = max(0, step[y, x] - 2)
            continue
        if u < holes + 0.45:
            L = int(np.clip(round(bh * rng.uniform(0.3, 1.0)), 1, 12))
            s0 = step[y, x] + (1 if lit[y, x] and rng.random() < 0.5 else 0)
            for k in range(1, L + 1):
                if y - k < 0: break
                step[y - k, x] = min(5, s0); mat[y - k, x] = m; band[y - k, x] = band[y, x]
    return step, mat, band

def paint_ground(W=320, H=200, ground='turf', landform='rolling', hour='golden', sun=None,
                 moisture=0.2, ppm=24, pitch=14, cam_h=1.7, canopy=0.0, canopy_scale=3.0,
                 shade=0.0, shade_scale=12.0, vignette=0.6, path=False, relief=1.0, sep=0.9,
                 sheen=0.5, contrast=1.0, jitter=0.5, band_z=(12, 45), seed=0, water=None):
    """ground: turf|meadow|earth|gravel|forest   landform: flat|rolling|hummocks|bank|ledges
    hour: noon|morning|golden|dusk|overcast (or sun=(az,el))   moisture 0..1
    ppm: px per metre at screen centre (the distance knob)   pitch/cam_h: camera
    canopy (+canopy_scale m): dapple from off-frame trees; shade (+shade_scale m): big cast
    shadows from off-frame forest/cloud -- Ferrari's compositional shadow masses
    vignette: darkens the bottom corners inside each family (repoussoir)"""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    land = Landform(landform, seed, relief, path=path, water=water)
    buf = render(land, W, H, ppm, pitch, cam_h)
    az, el = (sun if sun else HOURS[hour][:2])
    L = light(land, buf, az, el, canopy, canopy_scale, seed, shade, shade_scale)
    v, lit, sepm = value_plan(buf, L, hour, sep=sep, sheen=sheen)
    mat, wet = materials(land, buf, ground, moisture, L, seed)
    # moisture darkens the mass (a step at full wet) -- a mass-level change, not a texture
    v = v - 0.9 * wet * (v > 0.5)
    # repoussoir: bottom corners sink, inside each family
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
    vg = np.clip((yy / H - 0.55) / 0.45, 0, 1) ** 1.5 * (0.45 + 0.55 * np.abs(xx - W / 2) / (W / 2))
    v = v - vignette * 1.6 * vg
    off = marks(buf, mat, v, lit, rng, contrast)
    # distance: mark contrast compresses with depth beyond the near band
    fade = np.clip(1.2 - np.log(buf['z'] / band_z[0] + 1e-6) / 2.2, 0.15, 1)
    vv = v + off * fade
    # keep the families apart: marks may not cross the terminator (only a thin fringe may)
    vv = np.where(lit, np.clip(vv, 2.6, 5.99), np.clip(vv, 0, 3.1))
    step = dither.ordered(vv, jitter=jitter, rng=rng, n=6)
    band = np.digitize(buf['z'], band_z)
    mat[buf['sky']] = -1
    step, mat, band = crest_fringe(step, mat, band, buf, lit, rng)
    g = Ground()
    g.__dict__.update(mat=mat, band=band, step=step, v=v, vv=vv, lit=lit, wet=wet, buf=buf, L=L,
                      land=land, depth=buf['z'], wx=buf['x'], wz=buf['z'], h=buf['h'], sepm=sepm,
                      hour=hour, moisture=moisture)
    g.relight = lambda hr, g=g: colour(g, hr)
    g.rgb = colour(g, hour)
    return g

# output:
def canopy_field(x, z, cover, scale, sunv, seed):
    """Shadows of off-frame trees: soft union of crown shadows, stretched along the sun's azimuth
    (longer when the sun is low). Returns sun visibility 0..1 (continuous; the dither makes edges)."""
    if cover <= 0:
        return np.ones_like(x)
    r = np.random.default_rng(seed + 991)
    x0, x1 = np.percentile(x, 1) - 3 * scale, np.percentile(x, 99) + 3 * scale
    z0, z1 = np.percentile(z, 1) - 3 * scale, np.percentile(z, 97) + 3 * scale
    area = (x1 - x0) * (z1 - z0)
    n = int(np.clip(area / (np.pi * scale ** 2) * 1.2, 8, 700))
    cxs = r.uniform(x0, x1, n); czs = r.uniform(z0, z1, n)
    rad = scale * r.uniform(0.5, 1.4, n)
    acc = np.zeros_like(x)
    for cx, cz, rr in zip(cxs, czs, rad):
        dx, dz = x - cx, z - cz
        if np.abs(dz).min() > rr * 5 and np.abs(dx).min() > rr * 5:
            continue
        a = (dx * ux + dz * uz) / (rr * stretch)
        b = (-dx * uz + dz * ux) / rr
        acc = acc + np.exp(-(a * a + b * b) * 1.5)
    # threshold so that 'cover' of the ground is in shade; soft band gives dither in the edge
    thr = np.quantile(acc, 1 - cover) if cover < 1 else acc.min() - 1
    return 1 - 1 / (1 + np.exp(-(acc - thr) / 0.08))

def light(land, buf, az, el, canopy=0.0, canopy_scale=3.0, seed=0, shade=0.0, shade_scale=12.0):
    s = sun_vec(az, el)
    n = buf['n']; nm = buf['n_macro']
    X, Z, Hh = buf['x'], buf['z'], buf['h']
    ndl_macro = np.clip((nm * s).sum(-1), 0, 1)
    ndl_micro = np.clip((n * s).sum(-1), 0, 1)
    # terrain horizon shadow: march toward the sun
    vis = np.ones_like(X)
    hor = np.hypot(s[0], s[2]) + 1e-6
    tan_el = s[1] / hor
Bash: Fix canopy field and render
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('ground.py').read()
s=s.replace("""    rad = scale * r.uniform(0.5, 1.4, n)
    acc = np.zeros_like(x)""","""    rad = scale * r.uniform(0.5, 1.4, n)
    sx, sz = sunv[0], sunv[2]
    hl = np.hypot(sx, sz) + 1e-6
    ux, uz = sx / hl, sz / hl                        # horizontal sun direction

Bash: Step-skipping gap and stroke marks for grass
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('ground.py').read()
old=s[s.index("        bh = bh_m * ppm\n        rl = np.clip(bh * 0.9, 1, 14)"):s.index("    # earth: horizontal streaks")]
new='''        bh = bh_m * ppm
        rl = np.clip(bh * 0.45, 1, 6.5)               # measured: 1.6 far, 3.8 mid, 5 near (V16PM)
        o, t = runs_field(Hh, W, rl, rng, True)
        # tufts: tall narrow noise gathers darks into pockets and lights onto tuft tops
        tuft = smooth_noise(Hh, W, 3.0, 1.2, rng)
        from scipy.special import ndtr
        u = ndtr(0.55 * tuft + 0.83 * o)               # run quantile, tuft-correlated
        thr[sel] = u[sel]; coh[sel] = np.clip((bh - 1.0) / 2.5, 0, 1)[sel]
        near = np.clip((bh - 1.5) / 4.0, 0, 1)
        # MARKS SKIP STEPS: the dark gap between blades in light is a near-black green (step 1);
        # in shadow the blades are 'negative painted' olive strokes on the dark (step 2 on 0)
        gap[sel] = ((u < 0.13 * near) & lit)[sel]
        strk[sel] = ((u > 1 - 0.42 * near) & ~lit)[sel]
        # the mass's own mixture of base and one-step-down strands (density carries the turn)
        a = np.where(lit, 0.9, 0.5) * np.clip(bh / 3, 0.25, 1)
        off[sel] = (a * (u - 0.5) * 1.6)[sel]
'''

Bash: Materials by hour quick grid
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && cat > quick.py <<'EOF'
import ground, lw, numpy as np, sys, json
from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor
def cell(kw):
    kw=dict(kw); W=kw.pop('W',200); H=kw.pop('H',130)
    return ground.paint_ground(W,H,**kw).rgb
def grid(rows, out, k=3):
    with ProcessPoolExecutor(4) as ex:
        ims=[list(ex.map(cell,r)) for r in rows]
    pad=lambda a: np.pad(a,((0,4),(0,4),(0,0)))
    lw.save(np.concatenate([np.concatenate([pad(i) for i in r],1) for r in ims],0),out,k)
if __name__=='__main__':
    base=dict(landform='rolling',ppm=28,pitch=12,seed=4,shade=0.3,shade_scale=20)
    rows=[[{**base,'ground':g,'hour':h} for h in ['noon','golden','dusk']] for g in ['turf','earth','gravel','forest']]
    rows[3]=[{**r,'canopy':0.6,'shade':0} for r in rows[3]]
    grid(rows,'../stages/quick_mats_3x.png',2)
EOF

    \"\"\"Designed masses: no shadow island and no lit hole smaller than min_frac of the frame.
    Ferrari's shadow shapes are a few big ones; small isolated ovals read as holes in the ground.\"\"\"
    m = f < 0.5
    a = m.size * min_frac
    for val in (True, False):
        lab, n = ndi.label(m == val)
        if n == 0: continue
        sizes = ndi.sum(np.ones_like(f), lab, range(1, n + 1))
        small = np.isin(lab, np.nonzero(sizes < a)[0] + 1)
        m[small] = not val
    changed = (m != (f < 0.5))

Bash: LIC streaks for earth; path mass; render path study
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('ground.py').read()
s=s.replace("def marks(buf, mat, v, lit, rng, contrast=1.0):","""def lic(noise, phi, length, steps=None):
    \"\"\"Line-integral convolution: smear noise along the iso-lines of phi (screen space).
    Streaks then follow the form -- a path's length, a slope's contours, a dune's crest.\"\"\"
    Hh, W = noise.shape
    gy, gx = np.gradient(phi)
    tx, ty = -gy, gx
    nrm = np.hypot(tx, ty) + 1e-9
    tx, ty = tx / nrm, ty / nrm
    tx = np.where(tx < 0, -tx, tx); ty = np.where(tx == 0, np.abs(ty), np.where(tx < 0, -ty, ty))
    L = int(np.ceil(np.max(length))) if steps is None else steps
    acc = noise.copy(); cnt = np.ones_like(noise)
    for sgn in (1, -1):
        py, px = np.mgrid[0:Hh, 0:W].astype(float)
        for k in range(1, L + 1):
            iy = np.clip(np.round(py).astype(int), 0, Hh - 1); ix = np.clip(np.round(px).astype(int), 0, W - 1)
            px = px + sgn * tx[iy, ix]; py = py + sgn * ty[iy, ix]
            iy = np.clip(np.round(py).astype(int), 0, Hh - 1); ix = np.clip(np.round(px).astype(int), 0, W - 1)
            w = (k <= length)
            acc += noise[iy, ix] * w; cnt += w
    o = acc / cnt
    return (o - o.mean()) / (o.std() + 1e-9)

def marks(buf, mat, v, lit, rng, contrast=1.0, phi=None):""")
s=s.replace("""        rl = np.clip(0.5 * ppm, 1, 18)
        o, t = runs_field(Hh, W, rl, rng, False)
        thr[sel] = t[sel]; coh[sel] = np.clip((0.5 * ppm - 1.5) / 3, 0, 0.8)[sel]
        a = np.clip((0.3 * ppm - 0.5) / 4, 0.1, 0.7)
        st = stones_field(buf, 0.035, rng, density=0.18)
        off[sel] = (a * o * 0.8 + 0.8 * np.clip(0.03 * ppm, 0.15, 1) * st)[sel]""","""        # streaks along the form (LIC along phi's iso-lines), few and quiet; a few pebbles near
        rl = np.clip(0.25 * ppm, 1, 9)
        base = rng.normal(size=(Hh, W))
        base = np.where(rng.random((Hh, W)) < 0.25, base * 2.2, base * 0.5)   # sparse strong grains
        o = lic(base, phi if phi is not None else buf['z'], rl)
        a = np.clip((0.3 * ppm - 0.3) / 3, 0.25, 0.8) * np.where(lit, 0.9, 0.6)
        st = stones_field(buf, 0.03, rng, density=0.06)
        off[sel] = (a * o + 0.8 * np.clip(0.03 * ppm, 0.1, 1) * st)[sel]""")
s=s.replace("    off, thr, coh, gap, strk = marks(buf, mat, v, lit, rng, contrast)","""    phi = buf['z'].copy()
    if path:
        pd = land.path_dist(buf['x'], buf['z'])
        phi = np.where(pd < 2.0, pd * 40, phi)
        # the path's mass: a packed, lighter crown; grass-shaded margins drop a step
        onp = (mat == MI['earth']) & (pd < 1.2)
        v = np.where(onp, v + 0.5 * np.exp(-(pd / 0.35) ** 2) - 0.9 * np.clip((pd - 0.45) / 0.3, 0, 1), v)
        v = np.where(lit, np.maximum(v, 3.0), np.minimum(v, 2.9))
    off, thr, coh, gap, strk = marks(buf, mat, v, lit, rng, contrast, phi)""")
open('ground.py','w').write(s)
EOF

""","""    # the terminator of a cast shadow is broken by the material: blade tips cross it on turf,
    # grains on earth -- never a clean cut-paper curve
    Hh, W = L['sunvis'].shape
    rng = np.random.default_rng(12345)
    en = np.zeros((Hh, W))
    if mat is not None:
        nt = ndi.gaussian_filter(rng.normal(size=(Hh, W)), (4, 0.7)); nt /= nt.std() + 1e-9
        ne = ndi.gaussian_filter(rng.normal(size=(Hh, W)), (1.3, 1.3)); ne /= ne.std() + 1e-9
        grass = np.isin(mat, [MI['turf'], MI['meadow'], MI['moss']])
        en = np.where(grass, 0.22 * nt, 0.15 * ne) + 0.06 * rng.normal(size=(Hh, W))
    sv = L['sunvis'] + en * (L['sunvis'] * (1 - L['sunvis']) * 4 + 0.15)
    lit = (sv > 0.5) & (L['ndl'] > lit_floor * sy)
""")

# output:
from util import board

ROOT = os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood')
LIGHTS = {'left-front': (300, 40), 'right-front': (60, 40), 'back': (180, 22), 'high': (330, 68)}
HOUR_EL = {'golden': 10, 'morning': 22}
DIST = {'near': 40.0, 'mid': 16.0, 'far': 6.0}
PITCH = {'30': 30.0, 'low': 10.0}


def paint(length=3.0, diameter=0.35, orientation=15.0, rootwad=False, weathering=1.0, wetness=0.0,
          light='left-front', hour='noon', camera='30', distance='near', seed=0, pile=0,
          ground='gravel', W=160, H=96, grey=False):
    scale = DIST.get(distance, distance) if isinstance(distance, str) else float(distance)
    pitch = PITCH[camera]
    r = diameter / 2
    az, el = LIGHTS[light]
    if hour in HOUR_EL:
        el = min(el, HOUR_EL[hour] if light != 'high' else HOUR_EL[hour] * 2.2)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    rw = dict(n=6, end=0) if rootwad else None
    lg = geo.make_log(L=length, r=r, yaw=orientation, seed=seed, rootwad=rw,
                      ends=('snapped' if rootwad else str(rng.choice(['sawn', 'worn', 'worn', 'snapped'])),
                            str(rng.choice(['snapped', 'worn', 'splinter']))),
                      n_stubs=int(round(3 * weathering)), wet=1.0 if wetness >= 0.5 else 0.0,
                      n_knots=int(round(3 * weathering)))
    logs = [lg]
    if pile:
        extra = pilemod.make_pile(seed=seed + 1, n=pile, extent=(length * 1.6, length * 0.8),
                                  flow_yaw=orientation, r_range=(r * 0.4, r * 0.9))
        placed = [lg]
        for q in extra:
            placed.append(pilemod.settle(q, placed))
        logs = placed
    cz = float(np.mean([q.pts[:, 2].mean() for q in logs]))
    cx = float(np.mean(lg.pts[:, 0])); cy = float(np.mean(lg.pts[:, 1]))
    cam = geo.Camera(W, H, scale, pitch=pitch, look=(cx, cy, cz))
    li = Light(cam, az=az, el=el, overcast=(hour == 'overcast'))
    cv, buf, sh = render.render(logs, cam, li, seed=seed, stone_m=max(0.06, 1.6 / scale),
                                opts=dict(gravel=(ground == 'gravel'), weather=weathering))
    if grey:
        return render.to_rgb(cv, render.grey_ramps())
    return render.to_rgb(cv, palette.ramps(hour, 1.0 if wetness >= 0.5 else 0.0))


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ sheets
def _cell(args):
# Driftwood, after Mark Ferrari

Whole trees and logs stranded on river gravel bars: silvered, barkless trunks with root wads and
snapped stubs, piled against each other. Everything here is made by the programs in `src/`; the
stage images are in `stages/`, the parameter sheets in `sheets/`. Photos are in `refs/` (sources
in `refs/sources.tsv`) plus `~/work/pixelart-studies/transfer/refs/braided_driftwood_bar.jpg`.



src/run_all.sh                                # every stage image and sheet (~9 min, 4 processes)

"""Integration: the driftwood student's logs standing on this ground."""
import sys, os, numpy as np
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.expanduser('~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/driftwood/src'))
here = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)); sys.path.insert(0, here)
import ground, lw
import driftwood as dw, geo, render as dwr, palette as dwp, pile as pilemod
from paint import Light, GRAVEL

def logs_on_ground(W=240, H=150, hour='golden', ground_kw=None, seed=24, scale=40.0, pitch=30.0, pile=4):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    lg = geo.make_log(L=3.0, r=0.17, yaw=15.0, seed=seed, rootwad=dict(n=6, end=0), ends=('snapped', 'worn'),
                      n_stubs=3, wet=0.0, n_knots=3)
    logs = [lg]; placed = [lg]
    for q in pilemod.make_pile(seed=seed + 1, n=pile, extent=(4.8, 2.4), flow_yaw=15.0, r_range=(0.07, 0.15)):
        placed.append(pilemod.settle(q, placed))
    logs = placed
    cz = float(np.mean([q.pts[:, 2].mean() for q in logs])); cx = float(np.mean(lg.pts[:, 0])); cy = float(np.mean(lg.pts[:, 1]))
    cam = geo.Camera(W, H, scale, pitch=pitch, look=(cx, cy, cz))
    az, el = {'golden': (300, 10), 'noon': (300, 40), 'overcast': (300, 40)}[hour]
    li = Light(cam, az=az, el=el, overcast=(hour == 'overcast'))
    cv, buf, sh = dwr.render(logs, cam, li, seed=seed, stone_m=0.06, opts=dict(gravel=True, weather=1.0))
    wood_rgb = dwr.to_rgb(cv, dwp.ramps(hour, 0.0))
    wood = cv.mat != GRAVEL
    # contact: darkest ground pixels the log student drew right under the wood
    near = np.zeros_like(wood)
    near[:-1] |= wood[1:]; near[1:] |= wood[:-1]
    contact = (~wood) & (cv.step == 0) & near
    kw = dict(ground='gravel', landform='flat', ppm=scale, pitch=pitch, cam_h=4.0, hour=hour, seed=seed,
              sun=(20 if az > 180 else 160, el))
    kw.update(ground_kw or {})
    g = ground.paint_ground(W, H, shadow_mask=sh & ~wood, **kw)
    out = g.rgb.copy()
    out[wood | contact] = wood_rgb[wood | contact]
    return out, g.rgb, wood_rgb

if __name__ == '__main__':
    rows = []
    for hr in ('golden', 'noon'):
        a, gr, old = logs_on_ground(hour=hr, ground_kw=dict(shade=0.35, shade_scale=3, moisture=0.3))
        b, _, _ = logs_on_ground(hour=hr, ground_kw=dict(ground='turf', shade=0.3, shade_scale=3))
        p = np.zeros((a.shape[0], 4, 3), np.uint8)
        rows.append(np.concatenate([old, p, a, p, b], 1))
    q = np.zeros((4, rows[0].shape[1], 3), np.uint8)
    lw.save(np.concatenate([rows[0], q, rows[1]], 0), os.path.join(here, '../sheets/integration_driftwood_3x.png'), 3)
EOF

"""Labelled parameter sheets.  nice -n 10 python3 sheets.py [name ...]"""
import sys, os, numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor
import ground
HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)); OUT = os.path.join(HERE, '../sheets')

def cell(kw):
    kw = dict(kw); W = kw.pop('W', 200); H = kw.pop('H', 130); kw.pop('label', None)
    return ground.paint_ground(W, H, **kw).rgb

def sheet(name, rows, rlabels, clabels, title, k=3):
    flat = [c for r in rows for c in r]
    with ProcessPoolExecutor(4) as ex:
        ims = list(ex.map(cell, flat))
    H, W = ims[0].shape[:2]
    nr, nc = len(rows), len(rows[0])
    top, left, gap = 40, 110, 8
    S = Image.new('RGB', (left + nc * (W * k + gap), top + nr * (H * k + gap) + 20), (22, 22, 26))
    d = ImageDraw.Draw(S)
    d.text((8, 8), title, fill=(230, 230, 230))
    for j, cl in enumerate(clabels):
        d.text((left + j * (W * k + gap) + 4, top - 14), cl, fill=(200, 200, 200))
    for i, rl in enumerate(rlabels):
        d.text((6, top + i * (H * k + gap) + 6), rl, fill=(200, 200, 200))
        for j in range(nc):
            im = Image.fromarray(ims[i * nc + j]).resize((W * k, H * k), Image.NEAREST)
            S.paste(im, (left + j * (W * k + gap), top + i * (H * k + gap)))
    S.save(os.path.join(OUT, f'{name}.png'))
    print('wrote', name)

BASE = dict(landform='rolling', ppm=24, pitch=12, cam_h=1.7, seed=8, W=200, H=130)

def s_ground_hour():
    grounds = ['turf', 'meadow', 'earth', 'gravel', 'forest']
    hours = ['morning', 'noon', 'golden', 'dusk', 'overcast']
    extra = {'forest': dict(shade=0.7, shade_scale=4, canopy=0.2, canopy_scale=0.7, moisture=0.3)}
    rows = [[{**BASE, 'ground': g, 'hour': h, 'shade': 0.3, 'shade_scale': 10, **extra.get(g, {}),
              **({'sun': (80, 2)} if h == 'dusk' else {})} for h in hours] for g in grounds]
    sheet('A_ground_x_hour', rows, grounds, hours, 'Sheet A: ground type (rows) x hour (cols); rolling land, seed 8, ppm 24, off-frame shade 0.3', 2)

def s_landform_light():
    lfs = ['flat', 'rolling', 'hummocks', 'bank', 'ledges']
    suns = [(-60, 35), (0, 20), (60, 12), (85, 5)]
    rows = [[{**BASE, 'ground': 'turf', 'hour': 'golden', 'landform': lf, 'sun': s, 'relief': 1.2} for s in suns] for lf in lfs]
    sheet('B_landform_x_sun', rows, lfs, ['sun behind camera 35', 'sun left 20', 'sun left-behind 12', 'contre-jour 5'],
          'Sheet B: landform (rows) x sun (cols); turf, golden palette, no off-frame shade -- masses from land and light alone', 2)

def s_distance():
    ps = [(120, 35), (40, 20), (12, 12), (4, 8), (1.2, 5)]
    gs = ['turf', 'earth', 'gravel', 'forest']
    extra = {'forest': dict(shade=0.7, shade_scale=4, canopy=0.2, canopy_scale=0.7)}
    rows = [[dict(landform='rolling', path=(g == 'turf'), ground=g, hour='golden', sun=(15, 14), ppm=p, pitch=pi,
                  cam_h=1.7 * (40 / p) ** 0.6, relief=0.8, shade=0.3, shade_scale=12, W=200, H=130, seed=8,
                  **extra.get(g, {})) for p, pi in ps] for g in gs]
    sheet('C_distance', rows, gs, [f'{p} px/m, pitch {pi}' for p, pi in ps],
          'Sheet C: distance ladder (px per metre at screen centre); golden, seed 8', 2)

def s_moisture():
    ms = [0.0, 0.35, 0.7, 1.0]
    gs = ['earth', 'turf', 'forest']
    rows = [[dict(landform='rolling', ground=g, hour='noon', ppm=30, pitch=16, cam_h=1.7, relief=0.8, moisture=m,
                  path=(g == 'turf'), shade=0.25, shade_scale=8, W=200, H=130, seed=5) for m in ms] for g in gs]
    sheet('D_moisture', rows, gs, [f'moisture {m}' for m in ms], 'Sheet D: moisture (cols); wet ground drops a step, puddles in hollows take the sky', 2)

def s_seeds():
    rows = [[{**BASE, 'ground': 'turf', 'hour': 'golden', 'seed': sd, 'shade': 0.35, 'shade_scale': 12, 'path': True, 'sun': (15, 12)}
             for sd in range(1, 6)]]
    rows.append([{**r, 'hour': 'noon', 'sun': None} for r in rows[0]])
    sheet('E_seeds', rows, ['golden', 'noon'], [f'seed {i}' for i in range(1, 6)], 'Sheet E: seeds; rolling turf with a path and off-frame shade', 2)

SHEETS = dict(A=s_ground_hour, B=s_landform_light, C=s_distance, D=s_moisture, E=s_seeds)
if __name__ == '__main__':
    for n in (sys.argv[1:] or SHEETS):
        SHEETS[n]()
EOF
