Bash: Hero blades for near grass
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('ground.py').read()
s=s.replace("def paint_ground(","""def hero_blades(step, mat, band, buf, lit, rng, density=0.05, min_px=7):
    \"\"\"The nearest grass carries a few long single blades drawn as strokes (V13's foreground,
    V16's right bank): in light a dark stem with a lit pixel beside it (the paired stroke); in
    shadow an olive stroke on the dark (negative painting). Rasterized one row at a time so the
    lean accumulates into clean 1:1 / 2:1 runs.\"\"\"
    H, W = step.shape
    ppm = buf['ppm']
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(np.isin(mat, [MI['turf'], MI['meadow']]) & (0.2 * ppm >= min_px))
    if len(ys) == 0:
        return step, mat, band
    n = int(len(ys) * density / max(1.0, 0.2 * ppm[ys].mean()))
    pick = rng.choice(len(ys), size=min(n, len(ys)), replace=False)
    for i in pick:
        y, x = ys[i], xs[i]
        bh = 0.2 * ppm[y, x] * (2.2 if mat[y, x] == MI['meadow'] else 1.0)
        L = int(bh * rng.uniform(1.0, 2.3))
        lean = rng.normal(0, 0.35); bend = rng.choice([-1, 1]) * rng.uniform(0.2, 1.2)
        m0, b0, lt = mat[y, x], band[y, x], lit[y, x]
        fx = float(x)
        for k in range(L):
            t = k / max(L, 1)
            fx += lean + bend * t * t * 1.5
            yy, xx = y - k, int(round(fx))
            if yy < 0 or xx < 0 or xx >= W: break
            if lt:
                step[yy, xx] = 1 if t < 0.85 else 2
                if xx + 1 < W and rng.random() < 0.7: step[yy, xx + 1] = 5
            else:
                step[yy, xx] = 2 if t < 0.9 else 1
            mat[yy, xx] = m0; band[yy, xx] = b0
    return step, mat, band

def paint_ground(""")

s=s.replace("                 shadow_mask=None):","                 shadow_mask=None, blades=0.05):")

Bash: Absorb islands with their penumbra
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('ground.py').read()
old=s[s.index("def design_masses"):s.index("def light(")]
new='''def design_masses(f, min_frac=0.02):
    """Designed masses: no shadow island and no lit hole smaller than min_frac of the frame.
    Ferrari's shadow shapes are a few big ones; small isolated ovals read as holes in the ground.
    An absorbed island takes its soft penumbra with it (else a ring of edge is left behind)."""
    f = f.copy()
    a = f.size * min_frac
    for val in (True, False):
        m = f < 0.5
        lab, n = ndi.label(m == val)
        if n == 0: continue
        sizes = ndi.sum(np.ones_like(f), lab, range(1, n + 1))
        small = np.isin(lab, np.nonzero(sizes < a)[0] + 1)
        if not small.any(): continue
        grow = ndi.binary_dilation(small, iterations=4)
        if val:     # shadow island -> full light, including its soft rim
            f[grow & (f < 0.999)] = np.maximum(f[grow & (f < 0.999)], 1.0)
        else:       # small lit hole -> full shadow
            f[grow & (f > 0.001)] = 0.0
    return f

'''
s=s.replace(old,new)
open('ground.py','w').write(s)
EOF
OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 nice -n 10 python3 sheets.py F 2>&1 | tail -1

Bash: Composition plan bias for shade masses
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('ground.py').read()
s=s.replace("def canopy_field(x, z, cover, scale, sunv, seed):","def canopy_field(x, z, cover, scale, sunv, seed, bias=None):")
s=s.replace("""    # threshold so that 'cover' of the ground is in shade; soft band gives dither in the edge""","""    if bias is not None:
        acc = acc + bias * amp * 2.5          # the composition plan: where shadow is wanted
    # threshold so that 'cover' of the ground is in shade; soft band gives dither in the edge""")
s=s.replace("def light(land, buf, az, el, canopy=0.0, canopy_scale=3.0, seed=0, shade=0.0, shade_scale=12.0):",
            "def light(land, buf, az, el, canopy=0.0, canopy_scale=3.0, seed=0, shade=0.0, shade_scale=12.0, bias=None):")
s=s.replace("    sh = canopy_field(X, Z, shade, shade_scale, s, seed + 17)","    sh = canopy_field(X, Z, shade, shade_scale, s, seed + 17, bias)")
s=s.replace("    L = light(land, buf, az, el, canopy, canopy_scale, seed, shade, shade_scale)\n","""    L = light(land, buf, az, el, canopy, canopy_scale, seed, shade, shade_scale, plan_bias(plan, H, W, buf))
""")
s=s.replace("def paint_ground(","""def plan_bias(plan, H, W, buf):
    \"\"\"Screen-space composition of the big shadow masses (Ferrari designs them; V16: the right
    bank in shadow, the left lit, a pool of light on the islet; V09: a lit path in a dark floor).
      None | 'side-left' | 'side-right' | 'foreground' | 'pool' | 'V' | 'far'  \"\"\"
    if plan is None:
        return None
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
    u = xx / W - 0.5; t = yy / H
    ground_top = np.argmax(~buf['sky'], axis=0).mean() / H
    tg = np.clip((t - ground_top) / max(1 - ground_top, 0.1), 0, 1)     # 0 at horizon, 1 at bottom
    b = {'side-left': -u * 2, 'side-right': u * 2, 'foreground': (tg - 0.6) * 2.5,
         'far': (0.35 - tg) * 2.5,
         'pool': (np.hypot(u * 1.6, (tg - 0.5) * 2.2) - 0.45) * 2.5,
         'V': np.abs(u) * 2 + (tg - 0.8) * 1.5 - 0.3}[plan]
    return b

def paint_ground(""")
s=s.replace("                 shadow_mask=None, blades=0.035):","                 shadow_mask=None, blades=0.035, plan=None):")
open('ground.py','w').write(s)

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('ground.py').read()
s=s.replace("""        st = stones_field(buf, 0.05, rng, density=0.8)       # leaf litter clumps
        rl = np.clip(0.08 * ppm, 1, 6)
        o, t = runs_field(Hh, W, rl, rng, True); o = o * 0.4
        off[sel] = (np.clip(0.05 * ppm / 1.5, 0.2, 1) * st * np.where(lit, 1.0, 0.55) + o)[sel]""","""        # ground-cover leaves (V30): flat horizontal lozenges, lit top row, dark underside,
        # overlapping; bold in light, nearly gone in shade
        st = leaves_field(buf, 0.09, rng, density=1.2)
        a = np.clip(0.09 * ppm / 3, 0.3, 1.2)
        off[sel] = (a * st * np.where(lit, 1.0, 0.45))[sel]""")
s=s.replace("def lic(noise, phi, length, steps=None):","""def leaves_field(buf, size_m, rng, density=1.0):
    Hh, W = buf['z'].shape
    out = np.zeros((Hh, W)); ppm = buf['ppm']
    n = int(Hh * W * density / 8)
    ys = rng.integers(0, Hh, n); xs = rng.integers(0, W, n)
    for i in np.argsort(ys):                      # far to near: nearer leaves overlap
        y, x = ys[i], xs[i]
        s = size_m * ppm[y, x] * rng.uniform(0.6, 1.4)
        if s < 1.5:
            out[y, x] = rng.choice([-1.0, 0.8]); continue
        a = max(1, int(round(s / 2))); b = max(1, int(round(s / 7)))
        tilt = rng.normal(0, 0.15)
        y0, y1 = max(0, y - b - 1), min(Hh, y + b + 2); x0, x1 = max(0, x - a - 1), min(W, x + a + 2)
        yy, xx = np.mgrid[y0:y1, x0:x1]
        yc = y + tilt * (xx - x)
        q = ((xx - x) / (a + 0.5)) ** 2 + ((yy - yc) / (b + 0.5)) ** 2
        m = q < 1
        tone = rng.normal(0.2, 0.4)
        blk = np.where(yy - yc < -0.3 * b, tone + 0.9, np.where(yy - yc > 0.3 * b, tone - 0.6, tone + 0.2))
        out[y0:y1, x0:x1][m] = blk[m]
        yb = int(round(y + b + 1))
        if yb < Hh:
            xs_ = np.arange(max(0, x - a + 1), min(W, x + a))
            out[yb, xs_] = np.minimum(out[yb, xs_], -1.2)   # the dark gap under each leaf
    return out

def lic(noise, phi, length, steps=None):""")

"""Honest side-by-sides at 4x: Ferrari crop | mine, same native size."""
import ground, lw, numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
def crop(name, box): return lw.rgb(lw.load(name))[box[1]:box[3], box[0]:box[2]]
pairs = []
# 1 lit near bank (V16PM) vs my golden bank
g = ground.paint_ground(320, 200, ground='turf', landform='bank', hour='golden', sun=(25, 10), ppm=30, pitch=10, cam_h=1.7, shade=0.4, shade_scale=14, seed=5, plan='side-right')
pairs.append(('V16PM near lit bank', crop('V16PM', (0, 330, 150, 430)), 'mine: golden bank', g.rgb[100:200, 20:170]))
# 2 mid-distance masses: V16 far hummocks vs my rolling noon
g2 = ground.paint_ground(320, 200, ground='turf', landform='rolling', path=True, hour='noon', ppm=22, pitch=11, cam_h=2.0, shade=0.4, shade_scale=16, seed=3)
pairs.append(('V16 hummocks (mid)', crop('V16', (430, 218, 580, 318)), 'mine: rolling noon, mid', g2.rgb[40:140, 100:250]))
# 3 dusk terraces
g3 = ground.paint_ground(320, 200, ground='turf', landform='rolling', path=True, hour='dusk', sun=(80, -2), ppm=10, pitch=7, cam_h=6.0, relief=1.3, seed=7)
pairs.append(('V13 terraces + path (dusk)', crop('V13', (150, 260, 300, 360)), 'mine: dusk terraces', g3.rgb[60:160, 120:270]))
# 4 forest pool
g4 = ground.paint_ground(320, 200, ground='forest', landform='rolling', hour='morning', ppm=40, pitch=18, cam_h=1.7, shade=0.72, shade_scale=4, canopy=0.2, canopy_scale=0.7, moisture=0.35, relief=0.5, seed=21, plan='side-left')
pairs.append(('V09 lit moss path', crop('V09', (0, 380, 150, 480)), 'mine: forest pool', g4.rgb[100:200, 170:320]))
k = 4
rows = []
for a, A, b, B in pairs:
    pad = np.full((A.shape[0], 3, 3), 20, np.uint8)
    rows.append(np.concatenate([A, pad, B], 1))
    rows.append(np.full((3, rows[-1].shape[1], 3), 20, np.uint8))
img = Image.fromarray(lw.up(np.concatenate(rows, 0), k))
d = ImageDraw.Draw(img)
y = 0
for a, A, b, B in pairs:
    d.text((6, y + 4), a, fill=(255, 255, 0)); d.text((A.shape[1] * k + 18, y + 4), b, fill=(255, 255, 0))
    y += (A.shape[0] + 3) * k
img.save('../stages/compare_ferrari_4x.png')
EOF

Write: elements/ground/LESSONS.md
# The ground, after Mark Ferrari

What I was asked to fix: across the studio the ground is texture at a single scale everywhere, with no big value masses. What I built is a ground painter where **the masses come first, from landform and light, and texture lives inside them**. It works as a mass designer. As a pixel surface it is **not yet** Ferrari-grade at 8×, and I say where below.

Everything here comes from `src/`. Stage images are in `stages/`, labelled sheets in `sheets/`, and Ferrari reference crops in `ref/`.



cd src
nice -n 10 python3 sheets.py          # sheets A-F (4 processes, about 2 min)
nice -n 10 python3 compare.py         # stages/compare_ferrari_4x.png
nice -n 10 python3 s6_driftwood.py    # sheets/integration_driftwood_3x.png




---

## Stage 0: what Ferrari's ground is made of (read from his index maps)

I loaded his scenes as index maps (`src/lw.py`) and made squint maps (`src/analyze.py` → `ref/mass_*.png`), random-colour index maps (`ref/idxmap_*.png`) and 4–6× crops (`ref/crop_*.png`). What I found:

1. **Every ground passage is 2–4 big shapes before it is any texture.** In V16 there are four: the lit islet, a shadowed right bank, a lit left bank and dark far hummocks. In V13 the land is 5–6 terraces, each one a single dark value with a slightly lighter crest and a lighter path cutting across. In V09 the moss path is one lit ribbon in a black-green floor. Squinted, each of these reads as a flat poster shape.
2. **The shapes come from light, not from local colour.** The right bank of V16 is shadowed by forest outside the frame. Ferrari uses cast shadow from off-frame objects as a *compositional* tool (the analysis student found this too).
3. **Shadow masses are nearly black and hold very few values.** Measured in V16 (`L*`): the far hummocks are 2 / 6–9 / 25, the right bank 0 / 2 / 15–18, and the near left bank 3 / 10 / 15. In V13 the whole dusk ground lives between L\* 0 and 12. The light family is where the colours are.
4. **Inside a mass the turn is carried by an ordered 2×2 dither between adjacent steps.** On the V16 islet the checkered transition zone is textbook DPaint gradient fill: 50% becomes a clean checker, with a little jitter. I reproduced it (`stages/s0a_islet_6x.png`). Left is his islet. Middle is his value field put through my quantizer, which is indistinguishable at 6× once jitter is about 0.35 (`s0a_jitter_6x.png`). Right is my own radial field with his silhouette, which is close but has a rounder falloff than his flat-topped lozenge.
5. **Grain grows with nearness** (the master-copy student measured this, and I confirm it): 1-px checker stipple far off, then 1-px vertical strands 2–7 px tall, then drawn single blades in the nearest metre (the V13 foreground and the V16 right bank).
6. **Marks skip steps.** In lit grass the dark gap between blades is a near-black green, two or three steps below the base. In shade the blades are olive strokes on black (negative painting). This is where the contrast lives, inside the mass, and it isn't produced by the mass's own dither.

## Stage 1: masses from landform and light (the core of the technique)

`ground.py`, layers 1–4. The land is an analytic height field built from a few big features (rolling swells biased to run across the view so they stack into terraces, hummocks, a bank, ledges, a path). It is rendered by a column march, so every pixel knows its world x, z, height, normal and pixels per metre.

Light makes the masses:

- **Terrain horizon shadow**, marched toward the sun.
- **Off-frame shade** (`shade`, `shade_scale`). Soft unions of stretched crown shadows along the sun's azimuth, with a world-anchored ragged edge of octave sinusoids, so the shapes are lobed and notched like foliage shadows.
- **`design_masses`**: no shadow island or lit hole smaller than about 3% of the frame, and floating shadows must be bigger still. This single step did more against "speckle" than anything else. A mass is a *designed* shape, and small ovals read as holes in the ground.
- **`plan`** (`side-left | side-right | foreground | far | pool | V`) biases where shade falls, so the director can compose the masses as Ferrari does (V16: shaded right bank, lit left bank).

The value plan uses **two families that never touch**: shadow steps 0–2, light steps 3–5. The families are clamped both before and after quantization. The terminator is broken by the material: blade tips on turf, grains on earth. Inside the light family, grass responds weakly to slope (it's a forest of vertical blades that catches a low sun at any slope), while bare earth and gravel take full N·L. Inside the shadow family, form comes from **directional skylight**: surfaces facing the bright side of the sky are the "light" of the shadow family. At dusk that is all that shapes the land (V13). I exaggerate it with a tanh, since Ferrari turns land more than nature does. Then come the crest separation (land just behind a crest drops a step), receding sheen, and a repoussoir vignette inside each family.

See `sheets/B_landform_x_sun.png` (masses from land and light alone, with no off-frame shade) and `sheets/E_seeds.png`.

What I learned:

- A physical light model alone gives *either* nothing (a front sun makes everything one lit value) *or* too much (a low side sun makes everything shadow). Ferrari's masses are designed shapes. The fix was design_masses plus plan, not more physics.
- The 3.0/2.9 family clamp has to happen after the marks as well, or the ordered dither will mix a lit step into a shadow and the notan goes grey.
- The camera must stand `cam_h` above the ground *under it*. Otherwise the near ground can rise above the eye, and I got one seed whose frame was half a wall of dark grass (fixed).

## Stage 2: texture inside the masses, by material

Layers 5–7. Offsets in step space whose **size follows pixels per metre** and whose **contrast follows the family and the distance**:

- **turf/meadow**: vertical runs (length ≈ 0.6 × blade px, capped at 7.5), tuft-correlated so darks gather into pockets. Near: each run rounds to a whole step, so there are always two classes. Mid: ordered checker (V16). Near-black gaps sit in the tufts' dark pockets. In shade: olive negative strokes. A crest fringe puts blade tips over the land behind and dark holes along crests. In the nearest metre, a few `hero_blades` are drawn as leaning clean runs.
- **earth/path**: LIC streaks along the form (the path's length, the ground's contours), sparse and strong, plus a few pebbles. The path is its own mass: a packed, lighter crown and grass-shaded margins a step down.
- **gravel**: small stones with a light top row, dark lower row and dark pocket, deliberately quiet (1–2 steps in light, less in shade).
- **forest floor**: flat, overlapping leaf lozenges (V30's ground cover), bold in light and near-gone in shade.
- **moisture**: a mass-level darkening, plus puddles. Puddles are lobed pools in fine hollows that take the sky's ramp, with a dark far-edge band (the channel student's rule) and a wet rim.

Palettes (layer 8) are generated per material × depth band × hour from L\* targets, not from shading maths. The shadow albedo of green things is shifted to teal-green (measured in V16). The top light step goes ochre. The far bands lift the black point by an hour-keyed amount (at dusk the far land stays dark). `g.relight(hour)` repaints the same index canvas: `sheets/A_ground_x_hour.png`, `stages/palettes_16x.png`.

## Stage 3: distance

`sheets/C_distance.png`, from 120 px/m to 1.2 px/m. Strands hand off to checker at about 3 px of blade height. Mark contrast fades with log distance but keeps a floor, so far ground stays stippled rather than flat. The masses are what hold the image together at every scale. I did **not** run a 1.25× step zoom for pops (the grass-lod student's test). The handoffs are continuous functions of pixels per metre, so there's no switch to pop, but that is inference, not a check.

## Honest comparison with Ferrari (`stages/compare_ferrari_4x.png`, 4×)

| passage | verdict | why |
|---|---|---|
| V16PM near lit bank vs my golden bank | **close at 1×, not at 4×** | Value economy and strand scale are right. My palette is more olive and his is salmon-orange (a key choice, easy to change). My hero blades read as a drawn overlay. His near bank has none; they belong only in the front metre or on dark ground (V13, V16 right bank). |
| V16 hummocks vs my rolling noon | **not there** | His mid ground is *lozenge-shaped hummocks with a lit band under each crest and a dithered falloff*, a sculpted form turned by ordered dither. Mine is a flat shadow value with strand texture. My landforms are too smooth to make his hummock shapes; the hummocks landform exists but doesn't turn this way yet. |
| V13 dusk terraces vs mine | **masses right, surface not** | The terrace layering and path work. His terraces have a lighter crest band each, and the grass texture shows only on crests. Mine are more uniform. |
| V09 lit moss path vs my forest pool | **not there** | His light is a *ribbon*, 8–12 px wide, snaking. Mine is a broad pool with leaf texture that is too regular and too bright. Ferrari's forest light is narrower and darker, and it follows paths. |

The sheets beat the old studio ground on the stated failure: every cell is 2–4 designed value masses first. They do **not** yet sit beside Ferrari at 8×. The three biggest gaps:

1. **Sculpted mid-ground forms.** Ferrari turns every hummock and terrace with a lit crest band and a dithered falloff (the islet recipe, applied per landform unit). I have the quantizer and the islet copy, but my landform-to-value mapping at mid distance collapses to one flat shadow value. Next step: a per-crest "lit band" term keyed to distance below the crest line, to generalize V16's islet.
2. **Forest light shapes.** These should be ribbons and pools that follow paths (V09), not blobs.
3. **Hero blades on lit ground** read as scratches. They should be restricted to the front metre and to shadow.

## Using this ground (for cobbles, driftwood, willow, channel)



import sys; sys.path.insert(0, &#x27;~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src&#x27;)
import ground
g = ground.paint_ground(W, H, ground=&#x27;gravel&#x27;, landform=&#x27;flat&#x27;, hour=&#x27;golden&#x27;, sun=(20, 10),
                        ppm=40, pitch=30, cam_h=4, shade=0.35, shade_scale=3, plan=&#x27;side-right&#x27;,
                        moisture=0.3, seed=24, shadow_mask=my_cast_shadow)
rgb = g.rgb; g.depth, g.wx, g.wz, g.h   # world buffers to place objects
g.mat, g.band, g.step                   # index canvas; g.relight(&#x27;noon&#x27;) repaints




Parameters:

- `ground`: turf | meadow | earth | gravel | forest
- `landform`: flat | rolling | hummocks | bank | ledges, plus `path`
- `hour`: morning | noon | golden | dusk | overcast, or `sun=(az, el)`. az 0 = sun on the left, 90 = contre-jour.
- `moisture`: 0–1
- `ppm`: px per metre at screen centre; this is the distance knob
- `pitch`, `cam_h`: camera
- `shade`, `shade_scale`, `plan`: designed off-frame shadow masses
- `canopy`: small dapple
- `shadow_mask`: your object's cast shadow, painted as the ground in its shadow family
- `blades`, `vignette`, `sheen`, `sep`, `contrast`, `jitter`, `lit_base`, `seed`

**Demonstrated**: `sheets/integration_driftwood_3x.png`. The driftwood student's logs, root wad and pile (their `render` and palette, unmodified) sit on this ground. The left column is their original gravel. The middle column is my gravel with off-frame shade and moisture, and the right is my turf. The logs' own cast shadows pass through `shadow_mask`, and their contact pixels are kept. The difference is the point of the study: their ground was even speckle as loud as the logs, and mine is two big quiet masses with the logs as the brightest shapes. Caveat: inside a big shade mass a log's own shadow disappears (correctly, but compositionally you may want the log in the light: use `plan`).

The rules to take even without the code:

1. Decide 2–4 masses first, lit versus shadow, from off-frame casters and landform. Delete anything smaller than about 3% of the frame.
2. Keep shadow masses near black with 2–3 values and put the colour in the light.
3. Marks go inside a mass, sized by pixels per metre, and never cross the terminator except as a fringe of tips.
4. Use ordered 2×2 plus jitter for turns in mid and far ground, and runs only near.
5. Give contrast to the marks (gaps, stems), not to the mass.

Bash: Stage 0b: hummock crest-band copy
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && cat > s9_hummock.py <<'EOF'
"""Stage 0b: V16 far hummocks, copied by procedure.
His silhouettes (the index mask), my fill: per column, a value profile below each crest
(dark accent on the crest line, lit band ~25% down, falloff to a dark base), smoothed
across columns, quantized with the islet's ordered 2x2 + jitter."""
import lw, numpy as np, dither
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
d = lw.load('V16'); I = d['idx']; P = d['pal']
x0, y0, x1, y1 = 300, 215, 640, 280
S = I[y0:y1, x0:x1]
VAL = {69: 3, 235: 3, 72: 2, 59: 2, 70: 1, 120: 1, 126: 1, 71: 0, 127: 0, 60: 0, 61: 0, 62: 0, 52: 0, 53: 0}
RAMP = [71, 70, 72, 69]
m = np.isin(S, list(VAL))
H, W = S.shape

def crest_profile(t, accent=0.35, peak=0.25):
    """0..1 value shape below a crest, t = fraction of the way from crest to base"""
    up = accent + (1 - accent) * np.clip(t / peak, 0, 1) ** 0.7
    down = np.clip(1 - (t - peak) / (1 - peak), 0, 1) ** 0.9
    return np.where(t < peak, up, down)

def crest_t(mask):
    """for each masked pixel: rows below the top of its vertical run, and run length"""
    k = np.zeros(mask.shape); L = np.zeros(mask.shape)
    for x in range(mask.shape[1]):
        col = mask[:, x]; y = 0
        while y < len(col):
            if col[y]:
                y0_ = y
                while y < len(col) and col[y]: y += 1
                k[y0_:y, x] = np.arange(y - y0_); L[y0_:y, x] = y - y0_
            else:
                y += 1
    return k, L

k, L = crest_t(m)
t = k / np.maximum(L, 1)
f = crest_profile(t)
# smooth across columns so the band reads as a form, not per-column noise
num = ndi.gaussian_filter(f * m, (0.6, 2.5)); den = ndi.gaussian_filter(m * 1.0, (0.6, 2.5)) + 1e-6
f = num / den
v = 0.3 + 2.4 * f
st = dither.ordered(v, jitter=0.4, rng=np.random.default_rng(3), n=4)
out = P[S].copy(); out[m] = P[np.array(RAMP)[st]][m]
ref = P[S]
pad = np.zeros((3, W, 3), np.uint8)
lw.save(np.concatenate([ref, pad, out], 0), '../stages/s9_hummocks_5x.png', 5)
EOF
python3 s9_hummock.py

Bash: Add crest band and mass turn terms
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('ground.py').read()
s=s.replace("def paint_ground(","""def crest_profile(t, accent=0.35, peak=0.25):
    \"\"\"Measured on V16's far hummocks: value below a crest line, t = 0 at the crest, 1 at the
    base. A thin darker accent on the crest, the lit band ~25% down, falloff to a dark base.\"\"\"
    up = accent + (1 - accent) * np.clip(t / peak, 0, 1) ** 0.7
    down = np.clip(1 - (t - peak) / (1 - peak), 0, 1) ** 0.9
    return np.where(t < peak, up, down)

def crest_runs(buf, cap):
    \"\"\"Per pixel: rows below the crest line above it (an occlusion edge or the skyline) and the
    length of that run down to the next crest; runs longer than cap px use cap (near ground has
    no single form reaching the frame bottom).\"\"\"
    z = buf['z']; H, W = z.shape
    brk = np.zeros((H, W), bool)
    brk[1:] = ((z[:-1] > z[1:] * 1.25) & (z[:-1] - z[1:] > 2.0)) | buf['sky'][:-1]
    brk[0] = True
    brk &= ~buf['sky']
    k = np.zeros((H, W)); Ln = np.ones((H, W))
    for x in range(W):
        ys = list(np.nonzero(brk[:, x])[0]) + [H]
        for a, b in zip(ys[:-1], ys[1:]):
            k[a:b, x] = np.arange(b - a); Ln[a:b, x] = b - a
    return k, np.minimum(Ln, cap), Ln

def turn_masses(v, lit, buf, crest_band=1.0, mass_turn=1.0, cap=None):
    \"\"\"Turn the flat masses into forms, inside each family:
      crest band -- every land unit gets the V16 hummock profile below its crest line
      mass turn  -- a lit pool is hottest in its interior and falls off (dithered) toward its
                    edge (the V16 islet); a shadow is darkest along its edge and lifts inside
                    (reflected light), so no mass is a flat colour field with a noisy edge\"\"\"
    H, W = v.shape
    out = v.copy()
    if crest_band > 0:
        cap = cap or max(24, H * 0.35)
        k, Lc, Ln = crest_runs(buf, cap)
        t = np.clip(k / Lc, 0, 1)
        f = crest_profile(t)
        f = np.where(k > Lc, 0.0, f)          # beyond the capped run the form is done
        f = ndi.gaussian_filter(f, (0.6, 2.0))
        amp = crest_band * np.where(lit, 0.9, 1.3)
        out = out + amp * (f - 0.45) * (k <= Lc * 1.2)
    if mass_turn > 0:
        dl = ndi.distance_transform_edt(lit); ds = ndi.distance_transform_edt(~lit)
        D = max(6.0, 0.06 * min(H, W))
        out = out + mass_turn * np.where(lit, 0.8 * (np.clip(dl / D, 0, 1) - 0.5),
                                         0.6 * (np.clip(ds / (1.5 * D), 0, 1) - 0.6))
    return np.where(lit, np.clip(out, 3.0, 5.99), np.clip(out, 0, 2.9))

def turn_masses(v, lit, buf, crest_band=1.0, mass_turn=1.0, cap=None):
    """Turn the flat masses into forms, inside each family:
      crest band -- every land unit gets the V16 hummock profile below its crest line
      mass turn  -- a lit pool is hottest in its interior and falls off (dithered) toward its
                    edge (the V16 islet); a shadow is darkest along its edge and lifts inside
                    (reflected light), so no mass is a flat colour field with a noisy edge"""
    H, W = v.shape
    out = v.copy()
    if crest_band > 0:
        cap = cap or max(24, H * 0.35)
        k, Lc, Ln = crest_runs(buf, cap)
        t = np.clip(k / Lc, 0, 1)
        f = crest_profile(t)
        f = np.where(k > Lc, 0.0, f)          # beyond the capped run the form is done
        f = ndi.gaussian_filter(f, (0.6, 2.0))
        amp = crest_band * np.where(lit, 0.9, 1.3)
        out = out + amp * (f - 0.45) * (k <= Lc * 1.2)
    if mass_turn > 0:
        dl = ndi.distance_transform_edt(lit); ds = ndi.distance_transform_edt(~lit)
        D = max(6.0, 0.06 * min(H, W))
        out = out + mass_turn * np.where(lit, 0.8 * (np.clip(dl / D, 0, 1) - 0.5),
                                         0.6 * (np.clip(ds / (1.5 * D), 0, 1) - 0.6))
    return np.where(lit, np.clip(out, 3.0, 5.99), np.clip(out, 0, 2.9))

Bash: Add stage 4 to LESSONS; clean debug
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground && rm -f stages/dbg_* && python3 - <<'EOF'
p='LESSONS.md'
s=open(p).read()
add='''
## Stage 4: turning the masses (the V16 hummocks and the "construction paper" problem)

The coordinator's note on F_scenes was right: at 1× some masses, like the forest pool and the gravel-bar shadow, read as flat colour fields with a noisy edge. That is the construction-paper look. Ferrari's masses are *forms*: each one turns inside its family.

**Stage 0b copy** (`src/s9_hummock.py` → `stages/s9_hummocks_5x.png`). I measured the value profile below each crest line on V16's far hummocks. There is a thin darker accent on the crest, the lit band sits about 25% of the way down, and the value falls off to a dark base where the hummock meets the one in front. The checker is almost entirely between two classes, 69/70, which is a jump from L\* 6 to L\* 25. The middle step 72 is barely used, so the band is a *two-value* checker with a big gap. I used his crest lines (found as upward steps in his squinted value) and filled with my profile plus the 2×2 ordered dither on a 3-step ramp. The vertical rhythm matches. **What's missing:** his big hummock also turns *horizontally*, lit on its upper right and dark on its left flank. A 1-D profile can't do that; in my own ground it has to come from the geometry.

**Into the engine** (`turn_masses` in ground.py, parameters `crest_band` and `mass_turn`):

- **Crest band**: every land unit below an occlusion crest or the skyline gets the measured profile. A run cut short by the next crest ends in the dark base, and a long open run relaxes back to neutral after its band.
- **Convexity skylight**: mound tops take the sky and the feet sink. This gives the horizontal turn the copy lacked, from real geometry.
- **Mass turn**: a lit pool is hottest in its interior and falls off to its edge through dither (the islet). A shadow has a dark core just inside the terminator and lifts in its broad interior (reflected light).
- **The hummocks landform was rebuilt** as separate, flat-bottomed lozenges with the long axis across the view and flat ground between them. It was an overlapping bumpy mush that made no occlusion crests. Turf hummocks no longer turn into bare earth on their steep flanks.

`stages/s10_hummocks_4x.png` shows V16 on top, then mine without the new terms, then mine with them. The bottom row now reads as hummocks: lit bands under crests, dark feet, layered. See also the hummocks row of `sheets/B_landform_x_sun.png`, where the contre-jour cell rim-lights the crests.

**Where it stands:**

- The band's *value structure* is right.
- The texture is not. My band is a near-flat pale teal where his is a lively L6/L25 checker. The fix is a two-class ramp for the far shadow band, skipping the middle step as he does.
- The forest pool and gravel shadow now have a hot interior and a dark core edge. They are better, but the forest pool is still the wrong *shape* (it should be a ribbon, as in V09).
- I haven't re-checked these at 8× against V16.
'''
s=s.replace("## Honest comparison with Ferrari", add + "\n## Honest comparison with Ferrari")
s=s.replace("| V16 hummocks vs my rolling noon | **not there** |","| V16 hummocks vs my rolling noon | **not there (see stage 4 for the hummocks landform, now closer)** |")
s=s.replace("1. **Sculpted mid-ground forms.**","1. **Sculpted mid-ground forms** (partly addressed in stage 4).")
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF

Bash: Quantile-keyed shadow family
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('ground.py').read()
a="""    v = np.where(lit, 3.15 + (v - 3.0) * 0.78, 0.2 + v * shadow_key)"""
s=s.replace(a,"""    v = np.where(lit, 3.15 + (v - 3.0) * 0.78, v)
    sm = (~lit) & (mat >= 0) & (mat != MI['water'])
    if sm.sum() > 50:
        # key the shadow family by its own quantiles (a value plan, not a lighting formula):
        # the body of the shadow is the 1/2 checker, the crest bands reach solid 2, and only
        # the feet and cores reach 0 -- V16 hummocks: L2 15% / L6 35% / L25 38%
        q = np.percentile(v[sm], [3, 50, 97])
        tgt = np.array([0.35, 1.0 + shadow_key, 2.45])
        v = np.where(sm, np.interp(v, q, tgt, left=0.2, right=2.6), v)""")
s=s.replace("shadow_key=0.66):","shadow_key=0.55):")
open('ground.py','w').write(s)

Bash: Lit family keying and exaggerated turn
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('ground.py').read()
a="""    sm = (~lit) & (mat >= 0) & (mat != MI['water']) & ~buf['sky']"""
s=s.replace(a,"""    lm = lit & (mat >= 0) & (mat != MI['water']) & ~buf['sky']
    if lm.sum() > 50 and lit_key > 0:
        # key the light family the same way: whatever turning the land gives (N.L, skylight,
        # crest bands, convexity) is stretched to fill 3..5.9, so a lit meadow shows its
        # swells as sweeps of halftone and top light instead of one flat ochre
        q = np.percentile(v[lm], [3, 50, 97])
        if q[2] - q[0] > 1e-3:
            tgt = np.array([3.05, 4.35, 5.75])
            vk = np.interp(v, q, tgt, left=3.0, right=5.9)
            v = np.where(lm, (1 - lit_key) * v + lit_key * vk, v)
""" + a)
s=s.replace("                 shadow_key=0.3):","                 shadow_key=0.3, lit_key=0.8):")
# stronger in-family N.L for lit, exaggerated relative to flat
a="    v_lit = 3.0 + 3.0 * np.clip(lit_base + 1.0 * (r - 1), 0, 0.999)"
assert a in s
s=s.replace(a,"""    # exaggerated like the skylight: Ferrari turns land more than nature (tanh around flat)
    v_lit = 3.0 + 3.0 * np.clip(lit_base + 0.45 * np.tanh(2.5 * (r - 1)), 0, 0.999)
    # skylight also turns the lit family a little (faces toward the bright sky read warmer-lighter)
    v_lit = v_lit + 0.5 * (L['sky'] - 0.55)""")

Bash: Add stage 5 and voxsim note to LESSONS
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/ground && python3 - <<'EOF'
p='LESSONS.md'
s=open(p).read()
add=r'''
## Stage 5: the two-value far band, and keying each family (+ a note for voxsim's near-paint lane)

**The pale-teal band, diagnosed by measurement.** On the hummock test my shadow was 83% step 2, while his is roughly 15% L2, 47% L6 and 38% L25. There were three causes:

1. The far-band haze lifted the black point so far that steps 0 and 1 became nearly the same colour.
2. Skylight on flat ground put the shadow body right at the top of its family.
3. My grass shadow ramp had a small gap between steps 1 and 2. His has a big one: L6 to L25.

Fixes:

- The haze lift is now gentle on the darks.
- The turf shadow ramp is L\* 3/8/29, with self-occlusion darkening applied to steps 0 and 1 only.
- **Each family is keyed by its own quantiles.** The shadow's 3rd, 50th and 97th percentiles map to steps 0.15, 1.3 and 2.3. The result is 13/31/56% against his 15/47/38%. `stages/s10_hummocks_4x.png` now reads as V16's mechanism: bright crest bands in a big-gap checker over dark feet.

This is a value plan rather than a lighting formula: whatever turning the land and light give, the family is stretched to fill its designed range.

**Sweeps in a lit meadow** (prompted by voxsim `renders/near/st8/dusk.png`, whose sward has lovely long tree shadows but a flat lit field). My lit family had the same flaw. Grass takes a weak N·L, so a sunny meadow on gentle swells came out as one ochre. Fixes:

- The lit turn is now exaggerated around flat, as the skylight is: `0.45·tanh(2.5·(N·L/sun_y − 1))`.
- Lit faces toward the bright sky get a little lift.
- The lit family is quantile-keyed too (`lit_key`, default 0.8): 3rd/50th/97th percentiles map to 3.05/4.35/5.75.

`stages/s11_sweeps_3x.png` uses a high camera like theirs, golden hour, with off-frame tree shadows. The left column has none of the new terms; the right has all of them. On the right, halftone swales and lit rises sweep across the meadow following the land, and the shadows turn with the skylight.

Honest note: the effect is real but modest where the land is gentle (bottom row). At 8× I also see a ruled edge where a sharp ridge crosses a shadow (top right, the skylight tanh), which needs softening.

### For voxsim's near-paint lane (the terms, in world space, iso-safe)

All of these are per-pixel values computed from the height field. Apply them *before* marks, inside each family, then quantize with ordered 2×2 plus jitter.

1. **convex** `= tanh(2.5·(h − mean(h on a 3 m ring)) / 0.6)`. Add about 0.9 step in shadow and 0.5 in light. Mound tops take the sky, feet sink. Scale the ring to the land's feature size.
2. **skylight turn**: `sky = 0.55 + 0.45·tanh(6·(n·s_sky − s_sky.y))`, where `s_sky` is up tilted about 40° toward the sun's azimuth. This is the whole form language of the shadow family, and at dusk the only one.
3. **lit turn**: `0.45·tanh(2.5·(n·sun / sun.y − 1))` steps, even for grass. The exaggeration is the point.
4. **crest band** (screen space, below occlusion edges and skylines): the V16 profile. There's a darker accent on the crest, the band peaks 25% of the way down, and it falls to a dark base where the next unit occludes. In iso, every place the land hides land behind it is such an edge.
5. **mass turn**: lit pools are hot inside and dither down about 6% of the frame toward their edge. Shadows get a dark core line about 3 px wide just inside the terminator, then lift inside.
6. **key each family by quantiles** so whatever of 1–5 exists fills the family's range. Target shares near Ferrari's: shadow about 15/45/40 across its three steps; light centred on the middle step with about 10% top light.

Code: `turn_masses`, `crest_profile`, and the keying block in `paint_ground` (search "key the").
'''
s=s.replace("\n## Honest comparison with Ferrari", add + "\n## Honest comparison with Ferrari",1)
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF
grep -n "^## " LESSONS.md

The two-colour far band is in and I've sent the report. The flat lit meadow in the voxsim frame is improved too, but only a little where the land is gentle. I haven't checked any of this at 8×.

  • Far band: My distant hummock shadows were 83% one colour. Ferrari's use about 15% near-black, 47% dark and 38% bright. I found three causes:
  • Distance haze was washing the darkest steps into one colour.
  • Skylight pushed the whole shadow to the top of its range.

My shares are now 13 / 31 / 56% against his 15 / 47 / 38%. stages/s10_hummocks_4x.png now works like V16: bright bands under each crest, checkered against dark feet. - Lit meadow sweeps: My own lit meadow had the same flaw as the voxsim render. Two limits: - On gentle land (bottom row) the effect is small. - A sharp ridge crossing a shadow leaves a hard straight edge (top right) that needs softening. - For the renderer: I can only write in my own folder, so I changed nothing in the voxsim worktree.
